# Research Intake Tracker

The single place where every recommendation from Redlamp's research gets a decision, a phase and a status. It covers these studies:

- [AI and computational photography findings](ai-findings.md) (source tag **AI**)
- [darktable study](darktable-findings.md) (source tag **DT**)
- [Topaz-style upscaling and sharpening supplement](notes/H-topaz-upscale-sharpen.md) (source tag **H**)
- [Library and catalog findings](library-findings.md) (source tag **LIB**)
- [Tethered capture findings](tethering-findings.md) (source tag **TC**)
- [RapidRAW study](rapidraw-findings.md) (source tag **RR**)
- [RAWmakase study](rawmakase-findings.md) (source tag **RMK**)
- [RawRefinery study](rawrefinery-findings.md) (source tag **RRF**)
- [iPhone and iPad findings](mobile-findings.md) (source tag **MOB**)

Where studies recommend the same thing, the row is merged and cites each. *Last updated: 15 October 2026.*

## How to use this tracker

- **Every row starts as `Proposed`.** A row moves to `Accepted`, `Rejected` or `Deferred` only when the project owner decides, and the decision date goes in the Notes column. Items in the "Recommended" column are the research's suggestion, not a commitment.
- **Status** is tracked separately from the decision: `Not started`, `In progress`, `Blocked` (name the blocking row), `Done` (name the commit).
- **Change the tracker in the same pull request as the work.** A row is `Done` when the code, tests and docs land.
- **Review cadence:** walk the `Proposed` rows at each phase planning, and the `Blocked` rows weekly.
- **Adding rows:** new research or new ideas get a new ID in the right section; never reuse an ID.
- **GitHub issues mirror the work items:** each open row has an issue, kept in step by `scripts/tracker-issues.py` (decisions and skips stay here only). Plan here, then sync; issues people file come in as new rows. See `.cursor/rules/tracker-issues.mdc`.

**Columns**

| Column | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| ID | Stable identifier, prefixed by area |
| Item | What to do |
| Recommended | The research verdict: **Adopt** (take the idea), **Do better** (same capability, our own design), **Build** (no usable prior art), **Skip** |
| Phase | Recommended roadmap phase (P1 is now; P5 is iPad and iPhone, after 1.0) |
| Size | **S** ≤ 1 engineer-week, **M** 1–3, **L** 3–6, **XL** > 6. Figures in engineer-weeks (ew) come from the AI findings; sizes without figures are estimates made for this tracker |
| Depends on | Rows that must be done or decided first |
| Decision | `Proposed` / `Accepted` / `Rejected` / `Deferred` |
| Status | `Not started` / `In progress` / `Blocked` / `Done` |
| Source | Where the evidence is |

---

| DEC-99 | The seller's legal identity *(counsel)*: an individual or an organisation (with a D-U-N-S number); the copyright line (for example "2026 Pedro Gomes", where the app says "Redlamp contributors. Box with documentation, a phone number and an email address); where the seller is established, and so whether an EU (GDPR Art. 27) or UK representative is needed; and Apple's standard EULA or a custom one, and how either sits with MPL-2.0 | Declare trader status, since a paid app with tips makes the seller a trader; a P.O. Box keeps a home address off the product page; the identity, the copyright line and the licence agreement are the owner's, with counsel on the representative and on the licence agreement's fit with MPL-2.0 | ARC-24, ARC-27 | Proposed. The seller, settled by the owner (2026-10-15): his own account, an individual one, team 3JP75Z3F98, which already sells Lookout Station and holds Omoi Station, so the product page names Pedro Gomes as the seller; its certificates give the United Kingdom as its country. The copyright line, the trader details, the representative and the licence agreement remain open | [docs/app-store/compliance.md](../app-store/compliance.md#5-legal-duties-of-a-sale) |
| DEC-100 | The EU storefronts and the AI Act *(counsel)*: Article 50(2) has required machine-readable marking of generated images since 2 August 2026, and INF-11 §6.5 read that Redlamp may be the provider of Generative Remove's fill (RM-10); selling it under its own name in EU storefronts strengthens that reading. Do the EU storefronts wait for Content Credentials export (RM-03), or does the App Store build ship there without Generative Remove? | Ship RM-03, marking generated regions, before the EU storefronts open, keep the "Generated fill" label, and consider following the Commission's code of practice on AI-generated content; if RM-03 can't land first, delay the EU storefronts or leave Generative Remove out there | RM-03, ARC-27 | Proposed | [docs/app-store/compliance.md](../app-store/compliance.md#5-legal-duties-of-a-sale) |
| DEC-101 | Guideline 2.5.14 asks for "explicit user consent" and "a clear visual and/or audible indication" when an app makes a record of user activity, and the session journal writes recent activity, the photos opened and the folders shown to disk as they happen, with no way to turn it off. A setting, or a first-run notice? | A setting, with a plain explanation that nothing leaves the Mac unless a report is sent, mentioned in the privacy policy and the Report a Bug sheet | UX-52 | Accepted (2026-10-15): the owner chose the recommended answer | [docs/app-store/compliance.md](../app-store/compliance.md#1-app-review-guidelines) |
| DEC-102 | Guideline 2.5.9 rejects apps that "alter or disable the functions of … native user interface elements or behaviors", and the house rule removes every focus ring in every window (`FocusRings`, `.cursor/rules/focus-rings.mdc`), which Full Keyboard Access users rely on. Does keyboard focus show when Full Keyboard Access is on? | Show keyboard focus when Full Keyboard Access is on, and keep the rule otherwise; the guidelines audit rates the risk of a rejection low either way, and the choice also bears on VoiceOver and Voice Control | — | Accepted (2026-10-15): the owner chose the recommended answer | [docs/app-store/compliance.md](../app-store/compliance.md#1-app-review-guidelines) |
| DEC-103 | The App Store Connect answers: the secondary category (the primary is Photography, matching the app's `LSApplicationCategoryType`); the age rating's answers, User-Generated Content and the social media questions required since September 2026 included; and which Accessibility Nutrition Labels to publish once the app is live | Secondary Graphics & Design, or none; User-Generated Content No, since the public reports are support rather than the app's experience, and No to every other capability and content descriptor, giving 4+; Dark Interface first among the labels, each evaluated against Apple's criteria, the tip jar counting among the common tasks | ARC-27 | Proposed | [docs/app-store/compliance.md](../app-store/compliance.md#2-app-store-connect-requirements) |
| DEC-104 | Where do looks live in Develop, now that a photographer puts them first: the Base Look and film stock chosen at the start of an edit, then a preset or their own adjustments? DEC-74 lists film stocks, the firmware model's simulations and imported LUTs as recipes in the Recipes panel, and the README's first goal calls "presets, profiles and LUTs are all Recipes" a deliberate exception to Lightroom | A Base Looks panel in the left column, before Recipes, lists every look, with its own search: In This Photo, the photo's camera profiles, Redlamp's six, the Film Styles, the Film Stocks, the firmware model's simulations, Fujifilm LUTs and imported LUTs, and Reproduction; Recipes lists only presets. A recipe that carries a Base Look still sets it, as a Lightroom preset that includes a profile does, and its row shows it; a film stock brings its look and its own grain, halation and bloom (Black & White for a monochrome stock), and nothing else, and a look that isn't a film stock, chosen in place of one, takes them away; a colour look chosen in place of a monochrome one sets Color, as Lightroom's colour profiles do, and Black & White chosen on a colour look stays; Basic's Base Look menu stays as it is for now; Show in Finder and Move to Trash for the photographer's own and installed recipes and imported looks, and built-in folders hidden in either panel | UX-54, UX-55 | Accepted (2026-10-16): the owner's decisions, from a photographer's feedback on a call, and his answers to the wireframes' questions the same day (Color after a monochrome look); revises DEC-74's placement, keeping looks on the left with search and hover preview, each listed once | [Base Looks panel design](../plans/2026-10-16-base-looks-panel-design.md) |
| DEC-105 | How does a border relate to the photo, and where is it chosen? TON-25's Frame is drawn over the photo's edges (at its default size the 35 mm rebate hides about a quarter of the photo's height) and belongs to Effects, so applying a mood resets it, as a photographer pointed out | Around it: the canvas grows to the chosen shape (even, 1:1, 4:5, 3:4, 9:16, paper sizes) and nothing of the photo is hidden, as borders made with Lightroom Classic's Print to JPEG or export plug-ins are; chosen in Develop's own Border panel and a Borders folder in Recipes, apart from looks and moods; export can leave it out or add one to every photo. Text in the border and a blurred-photo fill are left out | TON-53, TON-54, EDT-35 | Accepted (2026-10-16): the owner's choice, from a photographer's feedback; on the wireframes the same day the owner put the Border panel last, after Calibration, measured Size against the photo's long side, kept the moods' names and chose edge print with the film look's name | A photographer, through the owner (2026-10-16); [design](../plans/2026-10-16-borders-design.md) |
| DEC-106 | Importing from Apple Photos (LIB-54, LIB-55): may Redlamp write to the Photos library, read Photos' own database or script Photos, and what does a photo edited in Photos bring? PhotoKit gives the originals, albums, favourites, dates and original names on macOS 26, and ratings, captions and keywords from macOS 27, never titles or people; Photos' edit data is an undocumented format, and Photos asks permission for every change an app makes | Photos is only read, through PhotoKit alone: never its database, never AppleScript, and nothing written back until EXT-05; a photo edited in Photos comes as its original with Photos' rendered version stacked over it, Photos' edit data left unread | LIB-54, LIB-55, EXT-05 | Accepted (2026-10-26): the owner's choice, with new photos in the chosen albums offered in the Library panel and a setting to import them automatically | The owner (2026-10-26); [design](../plans/2026-10-26-apple-photos-import-design.md) |
| DEC-107 | Do photos from Apple Photos stay in Photos, or are they copied into the photographer's folders? LIB-54 copies each chosen album's originals out, which takes their size again and leaves Redlamp's edits out of Photos; with Optimize Mac Storage most originals are in iCloud, and PhotoKit gives an app a photo's data and derivatives, never a path | They stay in Photos by default: the library refers to them by their Photos identifiers, shows them from PhotoKit's thumbnails and previews, downloads an original from iCloud when it's viewed at full size or edited, and keeps Redlamp's edits on this Mac; copying into folders (LIB-54) stays a choice | LIB-57, LIB-54, EXT-04, EXT-05 | Accepted (2026-10-27): the owner's direction, after LIB-54 shipped copying for 0.3.4; the same day the owner answered the design's eleven decisions with its recommendations: edits and metadata in Redlamp's store on this Mac keyed by the iCloud identifier, in iCloud Drive's layout; downloaded originals kept up to 20 GB by default, least recently opened removed first; browsing never downloads; Develop downloads the next photo ahead on networks that aren't expensive; Photos photos in All Photographs and searches, with a Source filter and an Apple Photos section; LIB-55 kept for copied albums only; Copy to Folder carries Redlamp's edits and the copy stands in; favourites read as picks, nothing written to Photos until EXT-04; a photo LIB-54 copied stands in for its asset; camera fields read when an original first downloads; a photo addressed by a path in Redlamp's cache named for its iCloud identifier | The owner (2026-10-27); [design study](../plans/2026-10-27-apple-photos-in-place-design.md) |

| TON-02 | Replace the tone map: asymptotic log-space curve, EV white point, display peak as a parameter, primaries inset, middle-channel hue preservation; Contrast, Whites and Blacks drive it | Do better | M | TON-01, DEC-09 | Accepted (2026-09-30) | Done (commit "Roll highlights off to white and keep wide gamut to the output"): identical to the old curve up to +1.6 EV, smooth shoulder to white at +4 EV; pure-white clipping at +1.5 EV fell from 19.7% to 2.3% (Fujifilm) and 4.4% to 0.04% (iPhone) | [DT §4](darktable-findings.md#4-color-science-and-tone) |

| ARC-02 | Viewport-only rendering (visible region plus margin, low-resolution whole image underneath while panning) | Adopt | M | ARC-01 | Accepted (2026-09-30) | Done (commits "Render any region of the photo", "Render only around the visible area when zoomed in"): at 1:1, engine render 5.9 ms instead of the full frame, 95 frames/s instead of 79, main-thread p99 9 ms instead of 34 ms | [DT §8](darktable-findings.md#8-architecture-performance-and-ux) |

It found Phase One's black levels and calibration left unapplied, a magenta cast (fixed in 09c00c4), and FFF files not opened as raws (0a763a9); Nikon's High Efficiency NEFs and the bodies LibRaw 0.22.2 doesn't list are CAM-12 and CAM-13 | [DT §3](darktable-findings.md#3-cameras-and-raw-data) |

## 3. Cameras and raw data

| ID | Item | Recommended | Phase | Size | Depends on | Decision | Status | Source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| CAM-02 | Measured per-camera white levels, plus clip detection that needs a spike of pixels at the maximum (replaces LibRaw's per-image heuristic) | Do better | P2 | M | CAM-01 | Accepted (2026-09-30) | In progress (1bb4b65): clip-spike detection replaces LibRaw's per-image heuristic (the Fuji sample's white had fallen to one stray photosite); the measured per-camera table waits on clipped sample shots per body (DN-05) | [DT §3](darktable-findings.md#3-cameras-and-raw-data) |
| CAM-03 | Apply DNG GainMap (OpcodeList2) by default in normalization | Adopt | P2 | S | — | Accepted (2026-09-30) | Done (7f2827b): OpcodeList2 GainMaps applied on the GPU after hot-pixel repair and highlight reconstruction, before demosaicing (linear DNGs per channel); Pixel 4a fixture added, corners 2.9x brighter. Noise reduction and the dual-demosaic blend scale the noise model by the gain per channel (a 64-texel field over the frame), so shaded corners are denoised as strongly as the centre: behind a 3x gain, 77% of flat noise used to survive where 54% did elsewhere; now the two are within 15% | [DT §3](darktable-findings.md#3-cameras-and-raw-data) |
| CAM-04 | Dual-illuminant color: interpolated DNG matrices plus ForwardMatrix | Do better | P2 | M | P1-01 | Accepted (2026-09-30) | Done: DNGs read ColorMatrix, CameraCalibration, ForwardMatrix, CalibrationIlluminant 1 and 2 and AnalogBalance, and render with them interpolated in inverse colour temperature (the as-shot temperature found by iterating the interpolation, otherwise the Temperature slider). ForwardMatrix is used when present, as the DNG specification prefers; without it, daylight matches LibRaw's matrix exactly. The Pixel 4a's ForwardMatrix is gentler than its ColorMatrix, so its saturated colours render noticeably softer than before. The Temperature and Tint model still uses one matrix | [DT §3](darktable-findings.md#3-cameras-and-raw-data) |
| CAM-05 | Paper-based Bayer demosaics (LMMSE, Menon 2007 or adaptive residual interpolation) instead of RCD and AMaZE | Do better | P2 | L | P1-01 | Accepted (2026-09-30) | Done (17aa648): Menon, Andriani & Calvagno 2007 from the paper, four GPU passes; beats Malvar by 0.15–0.6 dB colour PSNR on binned sample crops and makes 65% less zone-plate false colour. The paper's optional refinement step not implemented | [DT §3](darktable-findings.md#3-cameras-and-raw-data) |
| CAM-06 | Dual demosaic: blend a sharp and a smooth result by local contrast, with an automatic threshold | Adopt | P2 | S | CAM-05 | Accepted (2026-09-30) | Done (e89acb2): Menon's green gives way to the plain four-neighbour average where the neighbours span under 3 noise sigmas (fully by 6), threshold from the noise model; flat-noise green error down 19%, detail unchanged | [DT §3](darktable-findings.md#3-cameras-and-raw-data) |
| CAM-07 | Markesteijn X-Trans demosaic from LibRaw's CDDL source | Adopt | P2 | M | DEC-40 | Accepted (2026-10-07): the owner chose to port it under CDDL (DEC-40) and to change rendering for every X-Trans edit rather than gate it by process version, since it fixes a known gap in a pre-alpha | Done (02eaefb4): one pass on the GPU in bands of 256 rows (three passes measured no better), in two CDDL-1.0 files; it matches LibRaw's own output to 41–87 dB on the DN-11 test set. On noise-free X-Trans mosaics a sharp edge's MTF50 rises from 0.185 to 0.345 cycles per pixel (the lens gives 0.347), zone-plate false colour falls from 0.084 to 0.012, and photos gain 4.4 dB; with Luminance 50 it gains 1.8 dB at ISO 3200-like noise and 0.7 dB at 12800, equal at 51200, keeping 1.4 to 1.7 times the texture, and real RawNIND X-Trans pairs score the same binned. NoiseCalibration's X-Trans tables were re-measured (the test helper gave X-Trans 4 black levels for 36 positions; fixed), and the X-T3's process references re-recorded at every version: existing X-Trans edits render sharper, with more of their grain at low Luminance | [DT §9](darktable-findings.md#9-licensing-of-reusable-pieces), [DN-11 §5.2](notes/DN-11-lightroom-raw-denoise.md#52-demosaicing-alone-the-raw-details-question): the generic interpolation keeps half a clean edge's resolution, with ten times a learned demosaic's false colour |
| CAM-08 | CFA-domain highlight reconstruction (estimate clipped channels from unclipped neighbors) | Adopt | P2 | M | P1-01 | Accepted (2026-09-30) | Done (6789502): no clip at 1 after white balance; clipped photosites rebuilt from bright unclipped neighbours in cube-root space with a colour offset from the bright rim (constant-chromaticity Zhang & Brainard 2004); fully blown areas become the lowest consistent neutral. One-photosite detail against a rebuilt sky shows more demosaic false colour (CAM-05, CAM-06) | [DT §3](darktable-findings.md#3-cameras-and-raw-data) |
| CAM-09 | Segmentation-based reconstruction of fully blown highlights | Adopt | P3 | M | CAM-08 | Proposed | Not started | [DT §3](darktable-findings.md#3-cameras-and-raw-data) |
| CAM-10 | JPEG XL DNG reading via libjxl (moved from P4) | Adopt | P2 | M | — | Proposed | In progress (57ba38e): linear JPEG XL DNGs (iPhone ProRAW) decode through ImageIO rather than libjxl, mapped through the LinearizationTable; JPEG XL mosaic DNGs are refused with an error for now | [DT §3](darktable-findings.md#3-cameras-and-raw-data) |
| CAM-11 | Keep LibRaw for decoding; don't adopt rawspeed | Skip rawspeed | — | — | — | Proposed | — | [DT §3](darktable-findings.md#3-cameras-and-raw-data) |
| CAM-12 | Nikon's High Efficiency NEFs (HE and HE*, from the Z 8, Z 9, Z 6III, Z f, Z5 II and Z50 II), which LibRaw can't read: LibRaw#826's community decoder, fixed and held to a bench of CC0 samples, carried in Redlamp's LibRaw fork (CAM-30) until LibRaw's own decoder ships | Adopt | P2 | M | CAM-30, DEC-54 | Accepted (2026-10-10): the owner chose to carry LibRaw#826's decoder, fixed in place, in Redlamp's LibRaw fork rather than wait for LibRaw's snapshot; DEC-54 cleared it for a release on 2026-10-10 | Done (f94fc8f; the fork at 4abfcd2): HE and HE* files from the Z 9, Z 8, Z f, Z 6III, Z5 II and Z50 II open, other bodies' (the ZR's) are refused until a sample passes the fork's bench. [LibRaw#826](https://github.com/LibRaw/LibRaw/pull/826)'s decoder with RdWing's four fixes decoded the first five bodies' 22 files correctly; the fork fixed the rest: a layout per decode (two sizes decoding at once crashed it), band sizes and significance lengths as JPEG XS defines them (the fitted ones crashed the Z50 II's 3:2 and 16:9 files and garbled its 1:1 ones), failed precincts and malformed streams refused (20 of 32 fuzzing runs had crashed it). The bench holds all 28 HE and HE* files to recorded mosaics; against their lossless shots their colour balance agrees within 0.11% (the Z50 II) to 3.3% (the Z 6III, whose leaves move), and they pass the sanitizers, parallel decodes and 16,000 mutated files. The decode and colour suites have HE and HE* samples of all six bodies; the Z5 II's and Z50 II's colour references record them without a colour matrix, which waits on CAM-21. On a Z 6III HE* file posted with Adobe DNG Converter's DNG of it, the decode matches Adobe's in every photosite (fork b9fd8fc). Decoding takes about twice LibRaw's lossless time ([CAM-12 note](notes/CAM-12-nikon-high-efficiency.md#testing-libraw826-9-october-2026)) | [Camera coverage set](../../tests/decode/samples.json), [CAM-12 note](notes/CAM-12-nikon-high-efficiency.md) |
| CAM-13 | Move to a LibRaw release that lists the bodies 0.22.2 doesn't: the Sony A7 V is refused, and the A1 II decodes with a black strip along the bottom | Adopt | P2 | S | — | Accepted (2026-10-09): the owner chose LibRaw master's head over waiting for a public snapshot | Done (d12a989): LibRaw is pinned at master `7bfffe2` (9 October 2026), 106 commits past 0.22.2 with every fix of 0.22.2's stable branch (each has a same-subject commit on master; the rest are its version and changelog commits). The Sony A7 V opens through Sony's ARW6 decoder and passes the camera bench on a raw.pixls.us sample; the A1 II's YCbCr sample opens at 4332 × 2892 without the strip 0.22.2 left (which also turned and framed it wrongly), but with the magenta cast every Sony YCbCr ARW has (CAM-21). The 27 decode regression samples decode exactly as before, so no other body moved. Diagnostics and bench reports name the commit, "LibRaw 0.22.0-Release (7bfffe2)", since master still reports 0.22.0. Nikon's High Efficiency decoder isn't in master yet ([LibRaw#826](https://github.com/LibRaw/LibRaw/pull/826)), so CAM-12 waits on its own | [Camera coverage set](../../tests/decode/samples.json), [RMK §4.1](rawmakase-findings.md#41-raws-libraw-and-cameras) |
| CAM-14 | Camera bench: decode diagnostics (LibRaw's decoder, bits, frame and firmware; black level against optical black, the clip spike, dark edge strips) and a comparison of the default rendering with the camera's embedded JPEG (orientation, framing, structure, exposure, neutral cast, highlights), as versioned checks in a report format, with `redlamp camera-bench`; calibrated on the camera coverage set | Build | P2 | M | CAM-01 | Accepted (2026-10-04): the owner approved the camera bench plan | Done (9a56213, d318352, a83e03e, 6ed9782, 91c635d, 47633b6): each raw decode carries its identity (LibRaw's camera, decoder, bits, frame, firmware, previews) and measurements (black against the masked margins and the 0.1th percentile, the clip point, dark edge strips, the colour matrix); `CameraBench` checks them and compares the default rendering with the camera's embedded JPEG (orientation, framing, structure, exposure, neutral cast, highlights, colour difference), as versioned checks in a closed report format (`docs/camera-bench.schema.json`), with `redlamp camera-bench`. No check fails on the 26 samples of the 25 verified cameras, and five faults put into a Sony and a Canon decode are each caught by their own check. The run over raw.pixls.us (`scripts/camera-bench-seed.py`, 820 photos in 742 camera modes) found monochrome raws refused, bodies newer than LibRaw 0.22.2 opening wrongly, SuperCCD files crashing the decoder and more (CAM-20 to CAM-26, [note](notes/CAM-14-seed-run.md)); its false alarms set decode.black 3 and decode.edges 2 | The owner (2026-10-04) |
| CAM-15 | The Camera Bench window: photos or a folder, grouped by camera mode, a few photos per mode chosen to cover what the evidence still needs, results beside side-by-side pairs with the camera's JPEG, one question, and the exact measurements before sending | Build | P2 | M | CAM-14, UX-10 | Accepted (2026-10-04): the owner approved the camera bench plan | Done (5361ef1, 623b61c, 6626305): Help › Test Your Camera… and the command palette open the Camera Bench window: photos or a folder, grouped by camera mode, up to eight per mode for what each still needs, tested in an engine of its own through the decode service; each mode's checks, side-by-side pairs, what it still needs and one question; What's Sent shows the exact JSON; Report This Problem opens Report a Bug or Send Feedback under Photos & Cameras | The owner (2026-10-04) |
| CAM-16 | Camera bench submissions: measurements only, through `/api/bench` on redlamp.app, written by the Redlamp Feedback app to a private repo | Build | P2 | S | CAM-14, UX-10 | Accepted (2026-10-04): the owner approved the camera bench plan; results live in a private GitHub repo, and no raw files or pixels leave the Mac | Done (4cb46c5): `POST /api/bench` checks reports against the schema (a file name, GPS or path is refused) and keeps each as one file in the private repository as the Redlamp Feedback app, and `/api/bench/summary` serves what each mode still needs. Switched on 2026-10-05: the private pdcgomes/redlamp-bench repository, the app's access to it, and `BENCH_ENABLED` and `BENCH_REPO` on Vercel for Production and Preview; a real report was kept under submissions/2026/10/ and then removed | The owner (2026-10-04) |
| CAM-17 | Camera bench evidence on the cameras page: an aggregator turns submissions into a checklist per camera mode and the tiers DEC-28 decides, and publishes what each camera mode still needs | Build | P2 | M | CAM-16, DEC-28 | Accepted (2026-10-04): the owner approved the camera bench plan | Done (7badda5, 47633b6): `scripts/camera-bench.py` reads the private repository and the seed run, drops photos sent twice, judges older results again where only thresholds changed, and decides tiers by DEC-28 (proposed); `docs/camera-bench.json` and the cameras page carry them, 739 camera modes so far, and LibRaw's list marks them | The owner (2026-10-04) |

Sony's YCbCr ARWs (`sony_ycbcr_load_raw`, the lossless compressed M and S sizes: the a7 IV's [#480](https://github.com/pdcgomes/redlamp/issues/480) reported, the A1 II's, the RX1R III's, the FX2's) no longer open pink: LibRaw marks them and Nikon's sRAW NEFs as white-balanced by the camera (`as_shot_wb_applied`), which Redlamp didn't know, so it balanced them a second time. The decode now carries the mark (`balancedInCamera`), and from process 18 (raw revision 4, bc7897f06) normalisation leaves such files balanced once, as shot, so Auto finds the as-shot neutral on a grey card, and their noise is measured before the camera's balance ([raw pipeline §6 and §7](../raw-pipeline.md#6-what-a-decode-carries)). Edits made before process 18 render them as 0.3.3 did, balanced twice, until they're moved to Version 18 under Calibration › Process; focus stacks of them keep the fix in every version (the owner's choice, 26 October 2026). The camera bench, rerun on their samples at process 18 (daa2cb674), measures the RX1R III's cast at 0.5 and the FX2's at 1.2, from 17.3 and 17.0, with white highlights, so [docs/cameras.md](../cameras.md) reports both working; the FX2 passes every check, and the RX1R III warns only on framing and detail, as before. The rerun took every photo that failed the cast check: the Canon EOS D30 (9.6) and Sony DSC-F828 (14.3), whose files state no white balance (CAM-23), and the Nikon E5700 (19.2), whose file gives multipliers of 1, 1, 1, still cast. The Fujifilm X-E5's part is done, at the owner's request on 26 October: LibRaw's master has no colour matrix for it, so Redlamp's fork (CAM-30, `f4956c4`) gives it the matrix master gives the X-T5, X-T50, X-H2 and X100VI, whose 40 MP X-Trans 5 HR sensor it shares, and reads its colour-temperature table as theirs is read; the fork's change is drafted as a pull request to LibRaw. Its levels and crop already came from the file as the X-T5's do. Its CC0 sample is verified in the coverage set, its raws have an As Shot temperature (4,138 to 6,504 K on 13 donated files), and the camera bench passes it on every check. How bright it opens beside its JPEGs follows the exposure shift the camera records for its DR setting (by the median, 0.2 stops brighter than the JPEG at DR100's shift, 0.9 darker at DR200's and 2.7 darker at DR400's, on the donated files and the CC0 sample), which is CAM-35's, so it keeps the Fujifilm estimate rather than an exposure anchor of its own. The matrix renders from process 19 (raw revision 5, 3d337a5c9): an X-E5 edit made before renders as it did, from the photo as LibRaw opened it without a matrix (`rgb_cam` the identity, no `cam_xyz`, so no white balance of its own), until it's moved to Version 19 under Calibration › Process; focus stacks of X-E5 raws take the matrix in every version, as stacks keep #480's fix | [Camera bench seed run](notes/CAM-14-seed-run.md) |

## 4. Color, tone and detail

| ID | Item | Recommended | Phase | Size | Depends on | Decision | Status | Source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| TON-05 | Exposure-independent guided filter (EIGF) as the base for edge-aware Highlights, Shadows, Whites, Blacks | Adopt | P2 | L | ARC-01, DEC-05 | Accepted (2026-10-02): a self-guided filter on log luminance, once per photo at 512 px, behind process version 7; Whites and Blacks stay endpoints until measured ([design](../plans/2026-10-02-edge-aware-tone-design.md)) | Done: new edits' Highlights and Shadows (global and masked) weigh each region's brightness from the base, so the detail inside it moves with it; on ten photos they keep 0.70 of the shadows' texture against 0.65 per-pixel, and 0.95 of the highlights' against 0.88, with no visible halos, at 10 ms per photo for the map. Whites and Blacks stay endpoints (measured). Not done: a comparison with Lightroom's exports, and edge-aware Clarity (TON-06); shipping waits on DEC-05 | [DT §4](darktable-findings.md#4-color-science-and-tone) |
| TON-06 | Clarity, Texture and Sharpening on one multiscale decomposition, with deconvolution-style capture sharpening | Do better | P2 | L | ARC-01 | Accepted (2026-09-30) | Done: sharpening renders behind Lightroom's four controls, now noise-aware with deconvolution by Detail (SHP-01), and global Texture and Clarity render as gains on log-luminance bands between pyramid levels (about 1 ms). Masked Texture and Clarity done (26ca220). Edge-aware Clarity done (process 9, [design](../plans/2026-10-03-edge-aware-clarity-design.md)): the band ends at a self-guided filter's base at Clarity's scale instead of pyramid level 6, so at Clarity +100 on 13 sky photos the land beside the skyline darkens by 0.06 EV against 0.13, with the texture's boost kept (1.36), and the land's overall tone no longer moves (+0.02 EV against −0.08); 38 ms per photo for the map. One decomposition done (641d8ee, process 11, [design](../plans/2026-10-03-detail-decomposition-design.md)): four undecimated à-trous scales of the noise-reduced image's luminance per work area, read by Texture (a 0.25-stop soft limit, gain 1.6), Clarity (against its edge-preserving base) and sharpening's noise separator (Richardson–Lucy kept: as band gains it loses 0.1–0.8 dB). Texture's change beside strong edges drops from 1.0–1.7 stops to about 0.3 with the same boost on texture; Texture and Clarity drags 2.6–5.5 ms to 0.2–0.3 ms at 1:1 and fit level 1, 19–21 ms to 11–13 ms over a whole 24 MP frame; Luminance drags slower (5.8 to 9.7 ms at 1:1); the preview matches exports (ARC-04) except Texture's correlation at 700 px (about 0.8). | TON-09 | Native DCP support: matrices, HueSatMap and LookTable as 3D textures, profile tone curve | Do better | P2 | L | CAM-04 | Accepted (2026-10-02): split as Lightroom does, calibration underneath and the look as a Base Look; new edits keep Redlamp's default look ([design](../plans/2026-10-02-dcp-profiles-design.md)) | In progress: three fixtures embed profiles (the Pixel 4a Adobe Standard with a HueSatMap and LookTable, the iPhones Apple's tone curve). Slice 1 done: `DNGProfile` reads profile tags from DNGs and `.dcp` files, and process 4 applies the HueSatMap after the camera matrix, in linear ProPhoto, both illuminants' maps blended by colour temperature as the matrices are. It matches a CPU port of the DNG SDK's `RefBaselineHueSatMap` to half-float precision (2.5D and 3D sRGB-encoded maps); unlike the SDK, light above white keeps its headroom. The Pixel moves by mean ΔE2000 3.0 (worst patch 6.1), the expected Adobe Standard correction; its camera golden is re-recorded and process-4 recipe goldens recorded. Slice 2 done: a profile's LookTable and tone curve are baked into a 33³ scene-referred Base Look (dng_sdk's spline and hue-preserving RGB tone method; Redlamp's tone curve when the profile has none) and offered under "In This Photo" in the Base Look menu; new edits keep Redlamp Color, and choosing it keeps the baked table with the installed looks. Embedded looks never travel in exported recipes. Finding: Apple ProRAW's tone curve (0.18 to 0.12) follows its ProfileGainTableMap, a local tone map, and alone renders far too dark. The map now applies (process 5): ProfileGainTableMap and ProfileGainTableMap2 are read (big-endian, 6 × 8 and 12 × 16 grids of 257-point tables on the samples, gains 0.07–4.3) and applied in the develop kernel in linear ProPhoto after BaselineExposure, as one trilinear sample of a 3D texture, matching the specification's arithmetic to 0.004. Rendered under Redlamp Color it looked washed out (shadows lifted twice), so it comes only with the photo's embedded Apple look, as strongly as the look's Amount, which renders ProRAW the way the iPhone does; Redlamp Color renders ProRAW as before. `.dcp` import (slice 3) was deferred on 2 October, as the owner doesn't use DCP files, and moved to TON-51 on 14 October (DEC-85), which built it on 15 October (d0b8f5f) | [DT §4](darktable-findings.md#4-color-science-and-tone) |

| TON-11 | LUT import (`.cube`, `.3dl`, HaldCLUT) with log input spaces and an Amount slider | Adopt | P2 | M | — | Accepted (2026-09-30), pulled into the Recipes work | Done (fa86e4b): a Base Look table stage in the fused kernel (tetrahedral, after the tone map, mixed by the Base Look's Amount; about 0.1 ms at 1600 px), content-hashed `LookTable`s, and `.cube` (3D and 1D, DOMAIN), HaldCLUT and `.3dl` (Lustre/Flame mesh, 10/12/16-bit) import ([recipe format](../recipes/recipe-format.md)). LUTs made for camera log footage (S-Log3 with S-Gamut3.Cine or S-Gamut3, LogC3 EI 800 with AWG3, V-Log with V-Gamut, Apple Log; Rec.709 2.4 or sRGB out), each curve and gamut from the maker's paper, are converted at import into scene-referred tables that replace the tone map; Redlamp's own tone curve as an S-Log3 LUT renders like no LUT (ΔE*ab 0.44 mean). The input space is chosen in the import panel and with the CLI's `--space`. Not done: LogC4, Canon Log, N-Log, RED Log3G10 and ACEScct, and outputs other than Rec.709 primaries (F-Log, F-Log2 and F-Log2 C came with TON-43). Finding (TON-43): Redlamp Color renders scene middle grey at L* 64, Fujifilm's Rec.709 LUTs at 46–49, and camera-log import adds no offset, so Fujifilm's LUTs import with +0.9 stops; other makers' Rec.709 LUTs likely render as dark, not measured | [DT §4](darktable-findings.md#4-color-science-and-tone) |

| TON-17 | Halation: the red-orange glow around bright lights from light scattering back through the film base. Thresholded on scene-linear luminance, spread wider in red than green and not at all in blue; Amount, Threshold and Size; recipes can set it | Adopt | P2 | M | TON-06 | In progress | Done in the develop kernel: Halation Amount and Size, added in scene-linear light from a per-session glow source (`Glow.metal`). The source keeps highlights only, and gives near-clipped ones back up to 4 stops, since a sensor clips where film doesn't. Only light spilling past edges glows, so an evenly bright area keeps its colour. Radii are fractions of the long side, so preview and export match. Recipes set it; film looks use it (CineStill 70, others 4–14). Process 3 gives only small lights the clip boost: a large clipped area, such as a sky, is judged by its surroundings 32 times coarser, so branches against an overcast sky no longer glow pink. Masks add or take halation and bloom where they cover (Halation and Bloom in the mask panel; the radii stay global) | [Recipes studio](../recipes/agent-studio.md) |

Grain is sized to the frame (unchanged on a 24 MP photo) and is strongest in the low midtones and shadows. Grain Color decorrelates it per dye layer. The preview band-limits grain the way downscaling an export averages it, so preview and export agree (a test checks it). Film looks set grain from their datasheets' granularity | [Recipes studio](../recipes/agent-studio.md) |

Simulation-bracketing JPEG sets can fit a simulation relative to measured Provia. Recipe cards import through the `fujifilm-card` dialect. | TON-22 | Film and cinematic looks: scene-referred Base Looks (a look table indexed by log scene exposure that replaces the tone map), a physical film-and-print model driven by datasheet curves, and app looks measured with a capture kit | Adopt | P2 | L | TON-14, TON-17, TON-18, TON-19, DEC-19 | Accepted (2026-09-30) | In progress: scene-referred Base Looks render (the default curve as a scene table matches no look to a mean 0.5 levels). The film model runs on 13 stocks digitised from the makers' datasheets (`research/film-data/`), with colour masking. Negatives render through a print (Vision3 on 2383, anything on Endura or Multigrade) or through a modelled lab scanner, calibrated on a grey scale and a colour matrix as a lab does it; slides render directly. Finding: printed looks carry print-system contrast (about 1.7) and read too hard on a screen, so the everyday stocks use the scan. A catalogue of 11 looks (`FilmLookCatalog`, `redlamp recipe film --all`) pairs each stock with its rendering, a scanner profile (Frontier-like or Noritsu-like, characterised rather than measured), print timing, and grain, halation and bloom. The catalogue has 36 looks from 30 stocks, and all ship in the app: their tables are bundled as `redlamp/stock/<id>/look`, and their recipes sit in a Film Stocks group with golden renders. Seven are variants and processes: overexposed Portra, pushed Tri-X, Multigrade grades 1 and 4, bleach bypass, and cross-processing. A second batch adds Portra 160 and 800 (with a push to 1600), UltraMax, Pro 400H, CineStill 50D, Vision3 250D and 50D, Eterna Vivid 250D, Velvia 100, Ektachrome E100, Kodachrome 64, T-Max 100 and 400, Delta 100 and 3200, FP4 and Pan F. Kodachrome and Eterna come from archived manufacturer PDFs. Nine colour negatives without published dye curves fit their dyes to their own published neutral (Portra, Ektar, Gold and Superia moved to version 2 for it). 2383's sensitivity was re-traced, so its two looks are on version 2. The scanner reads absolute density and prints each frame by exposure, with its black point at the film base. There's a Film Looks window (`⇧⌘L`) showing the open photo in every film, and Redlamp-drawn film icons in the Base Look menu and browser. The README has a [film simulations section](../../README.md#film-simulations). Stocks with no published dye curves (Portra, Ektar, Gold, Superia) borrow Vision3's. The capture kit is ready ([design](../plans/2026-09-30-film-looks-design.md)) | This tracker |

The outer key is hosted beside the file. Both are on redlamp.app at addresses only release builds carry, from a file the owner keeps outside the repository with the secret; fetched over HTTPS when the toggle is turned on, kept encrypted on disk and decrypted in memory only, the plaintext wiped once parsed; releases keep the hardened runtime, so a debugger can't attach. Deleting either file or changing the hosted key turns the model off for everyone. `redlamp recipe firmware-pack` builds the file, the key and the kept file, and none is committed. A look goes over its base (Adobe Standard, unless it names another) and is baked at the edit's whole Amount by Camera Raw's rules: tables grow within their bounds, the curve is applied twice at 200, the RGB table applies at RGBTableAmount times the Amount; Amount 0 renders exactly as the base. Lightroom gives the Adobe Raw looks no Amount; Redlamp applies the same rules to them. Settings a look carries (Exposure, Clarity, split toning and the like) are left out and named in the menu's help, chosen on 15 October since Redlamp's sliders aren't Lightroom's (EDT-24). Where the photo has no edge to follow, a trust coefficient (the guide's variance against epsilon) keeps the mask's own. On a 4096-wide test with a mask at half the analysis grid, the 20–80% ramp narrows from at least 4 px to at most 2; on the Nikon sample the +1.5 EV of a Subject mask no longer spills onto the background beside the origami star. | MSK-33 | ViTMatte back on the GPU on macOS 27, with a model Metal Performance Shaders Graph doesn't abort on. On the build Mac's macOS 27.0.1, a ViTMatte prediction on the GPU at times aborted the process: preparing it, MPS Graph rebuilt the matmul at the compiled model's `model.mil` line 291 (global attention's relative positions, against an 8-bit constant of 64 × 64 × 64) with a 14 × 14 × 64 operand, failed its own shape check ("incompatible dimensions") and called `abort()`. The app makes the same prediction when it refines a Subject, Background or People mask, so it could crash for people on macOS 27. Meanwhile ViTMatte ran on the CPU on macOS 27 and later (6ad6842c8). Version 2 of the model, version 1 with its relative-position tables in float16, puts it back on the GPU everywhere, for everyone: each Mac downloads it once and removes version 1 as an outdated model | Build | P2 | S | MSK-32 | Accepted (2026-10-15): found in two crash reports from MaskRenderTests on the build Mac (14 and 15 October); the owner chose a row, with ViTMatte off the GPU on macOS 27 until Apple fixes MPS Graph, and sends Apple feedback with the crash reports. The same day, once version 1 with its relative-position tables in float16 no longer aborted (00f76b21d), the owner chose to replace the model for everyone: one model, the GPU everywhere again, the macOS 27 CPU path removed | Done (2026-10-15): ViTMatte is back on the GPU on macOS 27 without waiting for Apple. Version 2 of vitmatte-base (fa9c7cf60) is version 1 with the 24 relative-position tables its attention's matmuls take as constants in float16 (`decompress_vitmatte.py`; 111.1 MB against 108.8), published as the prerelease models-vitmatte-base-v2; each Mac downloads it once, and a launch removes version 1 and its compile as outdated (`OutdatedModelsTests`; `ModelStoreTests` downloads it from a copy and checks it against its manifest). The macOS 27 CPU path of 6ad6842c8 is gone (022491f99). On the build Mac's macOS 27.0.1, with eight probes at once, version 1 aborted on the GPU in 28 of 1,296 fresh loads, and in 1 of 152 beside the published version 2, which aborted in none of 480 (and none of 850 before; `vitmatte_gpu_probe.py`); MaskRenderTests passed in four runs there, three at once. On the GPU its Subject and People masks are version 1's bit for bit on the masking fixtures (seven masks with ViTMatte's strands, on macOS 26 and 27), 2.5 to 3.9 s a mask on the M4 Max against 5 to 12 s on its CPU, and the mask tests and the process gate pass on macOS 26 and 27. AI masks are stored with the edit and never made again as it renders, so no process version. App Store builds would get it as the asset pack models.vitmatte-base.v2, which waits on INF-07's App Store Connect set-up. The owner sends Apple feedback with the two crash reports | MaskRenderTests on the build Mac's macOS 27.0.1 (2026-10-14 and 2026-10-15): two crash reports |

| MSK-37 | Sky offers its models: picking Sky on a Mac without Segment Anything 2.1 or Depth Anything 3 offers the missing ones once, with their size together (416 MB for both), as Objects asks for its model; Not Now makes the mask from the classical estimate and isn't asked again on that Mac, and only picking Sky asks. Settings › Models and the manual stop saying Sky uses models built into macOS. Without the models Sky is the estimate, grown from the top edge, which never reaches sky that objects cut off: through `redlamp mask` on the evaluation set it left out 70% of the models' sky on sky-leafy-trees-01 (below a walkway), 56% on sky-wires-01 (between a pylon's struts) and 9 to 13% on three more | Do better | P2 | S | MSK-17 | Accepted (2026-10-27): the owner chose it after a Sky mask on a new MacBook Air with 0.3.4 left chunks of an obvious sky unmasked, with Depth Anything 3 offered beside Segment Anything | Done (025485c0b, f5814c47b, d2b918d2d, 524e22b49; [plan](../plans/2026-10-27-sky-model-offer-design.md)): `EditingEngine.modelsImproving(_:)` names Sky's missing models (Segment Anything 2.1, then Depth Anything 3, when cleared, published and fitting the Mac; none for a photo with an iPhone sky matte), and picking Sky in the mask picker or Create New Mask offers them in the Masks panel's download notice with their size together (415.7 MB). Download fetches each and makes the mask with them; Not Now makes it from the estimate and is remembered on that Mac. Settings › Models, Segment Anything's purpose, the manual and the README (769a83720) say what Sky is made with. SkyModelOfferTests and six new MaskEditingTests pass, and the whole suite, masking.panel-tools and the smoke tier passed on the build Mac. The offer itself hasn't been seen in the app, since the regression suite copies the models into its home | The owner, 27 October 2026 |

| EDT-23 | Lightroom Classic catalogs' edits, read-only: from the copy of the `.lrcat` LIB-29 reads, each photo's develop settings (read as data, never run as Lua) through EDT-11's converter with its report, written as the photo's Redlamp edit, and each virtual copy's as a version of its photo (EDT-09); LIB-29 brings the ratings, flags, labels, keywords and collections. The catalog and the photos are never changed | Adopt | P4 | L | EDT-11, EDT-09, LIB-29, LIB-48 | Accepted (2026-10-13): the owner's choice, with EDT-12 and EDT-09, after a photographer who has used Lightroom since its first version asked for their catalog; the same day the owner chose measured sliders as how close the edits come (EDT-24), Process 2003 and 2010 converted (EDT-31), and a photo already edited in Redlamp keeping its edit, with Lightroom's added as a version ([design](../plans/2026-10-13-lightroom-edits-design.md#decisions-2026-10-13-the-owner)). On 14 October the owner aimed it at 0.3.3, the release after 0.3.2, with EDT-33, EDT-34 and versions (EDT-09, LIB-48): everything but masks and spots, which come later ([RMK §9.7](rawmakase-findings.md#97-recommendations)). On 16 October the owner split it: 0.3.3 brings a catalog's photo edits, with EDT-12's XMP sidecars, EDT-33 and EDT-34, while virtual copies stay listed as left behind and a photo already edited in Redlamp keeps its edit with Lightroom's reported; virtual copies as versions follow in 0.3.4, with EDT-09 and LIB-48 | In progress (649ac7363 to a8e134918, 66f53ae9a to e913d999f, 231e44982 to c9889d641, 7e5142a1c, 35dae43d0 to 8af2a8b88, 096e3b1d6 to 1a121f7b4): built: one settings model for the catalog's Lua and for XMP (`CameraRawSettings`, in RedlampRecipes), keeping nested structures and arrays of them to any depth (a mask group's corrections and their masks, spots and their masks, the profile's parameters, Upright's guides), read alike from XMP in every RDF form and from the catalog's Lua tables, read as data (`LuaTable`, now in RedlampRecipes, with RedlampLibrary depending on it); 100,000 photos' settings parse in 0.81 s on the build Mac's 16 cores. The catalog reader reads each photo's and virtual copy's Develop settings, Lightroom's rotation code, the file's size as the catalog records it, its snapshots and the number of its history steps, in parts of 500 on every core while the catalog is open ([format note](notes/EDT-lightroom-develop-settings.md)). `redlamp library lightroom <catalog> --census` counts what the edits use, naming no photo, path, keyword or value: photos by process version, setting, mask component, spot kind, profile and look, and Upright mode, the photos beyond 16 masks or 64 components, snapshots and history steps, and each rotation code with the EXIF orientations of the files the library has; 1.17 s at 100,000 photos. A photo's settings, from the catalog's Lua or from XMP, become its whole edit through EDT-11's table (`LightroomPhoto.convert`), masks and spots aside: each setting mapped, approximated or left out with its reason (not yet checked against Lightroom, an earlier process version, not in Redlamp with the row that would bring it, unreadable or unknown), the profile as the design maps it, and the edit's provenance; a test pins each row of the design's table, the crop is checked through `GeometryMap` for every camera and catalog orientation, and 100,000 photos read and convert in 1.27 s. Each converted edit records where it came from, as the recipe's optional `provenance` key in the sidecar format: the catalog or the XMP file, Lightroom's photo and its settings' digest, the date, and the settings approximated or left out (7e5142a1c). File › Import from Lightroom Classic… and `redlamp library lightroom` bring each photo's edit and snapshots with LIB-29's fields. The report's Edits section, before anything is written, in the window and the CLI's text and JSON, says how fully each photo converts, why settings are left out, the photos by process version, and what stays behind (history steps); each photo's sidecar is written once, with its keywords, edit and snapshots; a photo already edited in Redlamp keeps its edit, with Lightroom's added as a version named Lightroom Classic; the photo open in Develop takes its edit there; Undo Import takes it all back. Each virtual copy comes across as a version of its photo (EDT-09) in the same write, named as in Lightroom, with its own converted edit, snapshots, rating, pick, label, keywords, title and caption; a copy brought before from the same catalog is left as it is and counted; the photo open in Develop takes its versions there; Undo Import, and the CLI's `--undo`, take back only the versions the import added (096e3b1d6 to 1a121f7b4). Lightroom's rotation codes are inferred from the catalog: for each EXIF orientation, the code most of its photos carry counts as not turned, and only photos turned in Lightroom are reported, with nothing applied. A raw names the photographer's own Adobe profile where it's on the Mac (EDT-33), and a Fujifilm simulation the look that fills its film slot. 100,000 photos: the report in 3.90 s, the import in 94.5 s and Undo in 112.6 s, on the build Mac at loads of 11 to 14; with 5,000 virtual copies, the report in 3.96 to 4.07 s, the import in 67.7 to 77.8 s and Undo in 127.6 to 128.4 s, at loads of 17 to 26 (scenario `library.lightroom-edits`). Not done ([design](../plans/2026-10-13-lightroom-edits-design.md)): the census of a real catalog, which settles the codes of photos turned in Lightroom; Show Photos for each reason (LIB-52); the write's speed with edits, and on spinning disks (LIB-53); Undo Import's rule for a photo with one of its versions open in Develop, which it leaves alone, and which takes Convert Lightroom Edits' rule after LIB-48 (its own edit put back, the version keeping its own), as the owner chose on 26 October. What only files Lightroom made can settle (where masks and spots sit, Upright's modes, panel switches) is left out of imports until a test catalog checks it, which waits until the owner has help. EDT-11 converts presets of Process 2012 and later and refuses earlier ones | [RMK §5.4](rawmakase-findings.md#54-lightroom-classic-catalog-import-lesson-4) |

## 7. Lenses and geometry

| ID | Item | Recommended | Phase | Size | Depends on | Decision | Status | Source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| LNS-01 | One lens-correction engine: every source baked into per-image 1-D tables (distortion per channel, vignetting, optical center, tangential terms) | Do better | P2 | L | — | Accepted (2026-10-02): the owner asked for lens profile corrections | In progress: `LensCorrection` holds every source as tables over the radius from the optical centre (red, green and blue's recorded scale, the vignetting gain). `GeometryMap` applies it after the manual Distortion slider (overlays, work areas and Constrain to Image included), zoomed just enough to keep the frame filled when a correction pulls the edges in, and the develop kernel reads it as a 64-entry table, sampling red and blue apart where the profile corrects colour fringes. A synthetic render matches the CPU geometry to 0.0008 on average. Tangential terms aren't carried (they don't fit a radial table; phones write zero) | [DT §7](darktable-findings.md#7-lenses-and-geometry) |
| LNS-02 | Embedded manufacturer corrections (Sony, Fujifilm, OM System, Panasonic, DNG opcodes), moved from P3; validated against in-camera JPEGs | Adopt | P2 | M | LNS-01 | Accepted (2026-10-02): taken instead of lensfun while DEC-04 waits on counsel; on 13 October the owner chose its Panasonic and OM System corrections for 0.3.2, in one new process version with CAM-29 and DN-12, which LNS-14 and LNS-16 joined on 14 October | Done (commit "Panasonic's and OM System's distortion corrections from process 16", the other makers' in processes 5 and 6): DNG OpcodeList3 WarpRectilinear and FixVignetteRadial (from the specification) and Sony's ARW tags 0x7032, 0x7035 and 0x7037 (the conventions darktable and RawTherapee use) apply by default from process 5 (Enable Profile Corrections, with Distortion and Vignetting amounts). The Sony sample moves by mean ΔE2000 3.3, and its frame edge now sits where the camera's JPEG has it (39% across a corner crop, against 40% corrected and 43% before); the Pixel's warp is the identity. Fujifilm's RAF tags (0xF00B, 0xF00F and 0xF010 in the FujiIFD, 9 or 11 knots) follow from process 6, since process 5 had already shipped: on the X-T2 sample they correct a 9% barrel at the corners, and the corrected render matches the camera's JPEG with an edge correlation of 0.93 (0.62 uncorrected; every other sign or radius convention 0.69 at best). Its fringe tables agree with Remove Chromatic Aberration's measurement of the same photo (red −0.0013 against −0.0018, blue +0.0016 against +0.0015). Panasonic's and OM System's distortion follows from process 16, worked out by matching each raw.pixls.us camera's JPEG to its uncorrected raw point by point, since neither maker documents its tags: the RW2's tag 0x0119 corrects a recorded radius r to r(1 + (w₈r² + w₄r⁴ + w₁₁r⁶)·2⁻¹⁵)/(1 + w₅·2⁻¹⁵), in word 12's half-diagonal about the active area's centre (within 1% of the best free radial fit over 89 cameras; the next assignment of words 1.37 times it), and the ORF's ImageProcessing tag 0x150A records a corrected radius r at x(1 + k₁x² + k₂x⁴ + k₃x⁶), x = s·r, in the camera crop's half-diagonal (within 0.2% over 34 cameras; s outside the polynomial, or LibRaw's frame for the radius, fit worse). On the camera bench, the 94 Panasonic samples with a correction (Leica's RWLs from Panasonic-made cameras among them) go from framing a median 1.8% wider than their JPEGs to 0.25% (edge correlation 0.87 to 0.95, up on 93 and down on none), and the 31 Olympus and OM System ones from 2.5% to 0.5%, the margin LibRaw keeps beyond the camera's crop (correlation 0.84 to 0.94, up on 28); the S5 II sample frames at 1.000 (correlation 0.94 to 0.99) and the OM-1 Mark II at 1.005 (0.84 to 0.87). Their chromatic aberration and shading, and Fujifilm's 1.25× crop modes, went to LNS-18 on 15 October | [DT §7](darktable-findings.md#7-lenses-and-geometry), [the models' scripts](../../research/lens-corrections/lns-02/) |
| LNS-03 | lensfun models reimplemented from their published math; database shipped as data | Adopt | P2 | M | LNS-01, DEC-04 | Proposed | Not started | [DT §7](darktable-findings.md#7-lenses-and-geometry) |
| LNS-04 | Import user-supplied Adobe LCP files with our own reader; never ship or convert Adobe's | Adopt | P2 | S | LNS-01 | Accepted (2026-10-03): the owner approved it for agent wave 1 | Done (0c8729c, 45e252c, 62c8574): our own reader for the LCP XML (both RDF forms) and the Adobe Camera Model's rectilinear distortion, lateral chromatic aberration and vignetting (Chen et al., 2010), tabulated into `LensCorrection` with source `profile`; profiles in `~/Library/Application Support/Redlamp/Lens Profiles/` are matched by camera and lens (EXIF and LibRaw spellings, then the lens's display name) and interpolated in focal length and aperture. A correction the file carries wins; a crop mode's frame (shape or size off the profile's) gets none; it applies from process 5, under the profile-corrections switch, in the app and CLI only (engine tests never read a developer's profiles). Not done: fisheye profiles (LNS-17), tangential terms and focus-distance interpolation; the matched profile's name came with LNS-11. Up to process 16 each profile is used only on the body it was made on; from process 17 profiles made on other bodies (LNS-14) and those in Camera Raw's folders (LNS-16) too, and one can be chosen by hand (LNS-15) | [DT §9](darktable-findings.md#9-licensing-of-reusable-pieces) |
| LNS-05 | All geometry (crop, Transform, orientation, lens) as one inverse map, sampling the pyramid once | Do better | P2 | L | LNS-01, MSK-01 | Accepted (2026-10-01) | In progress: `GeometryMap` composes crop with its angle, the manual Transform (a virtual camera tilted, turned and rolled, then stretched, scaled and offset), and the user's rotation and flip into one homography from the developed frame to the photo; the develop kernel applies it per pixel and samples the pyramid once, at the level its Jacobian picks, and white fills where no photo remains. Constrain to Image shrinks the crop about its centre until it fits. Manual lens Distortion is one radial polynomial after the homography (the renderer needs only the forward model; overlays invert it by Newton steps), and manual Vignetting is a gain on the recorded photo; profile and embedded corrections will feed the same step | [DT §7](darktable-findings.md#7-lenses-and-geometry) |
| LNS-06 | Crop and guides copied from Lightroom, parameters mapping to Lightroom's crop fields; post-crop vignette follows the crop | Adopt | P2 | M | LNS-05 | Accepted (2026-10-01) | Done: the Crop & Straighten tool (R) shows the whole straightened frame with the crop over it (thirds grid, corner and edge handles, drag to move), aspect presets with a lock (A), the Angle slider, Constrain to Image (which fits the crop as drawn again after every Angle or Transform change), rotate (⌘[ / ⌘]) and flip, and Reset; vignette and grain follow the crop. Overlays (Grid, Thirds, Diagonal, Golden Triangle, Golden Ratio, Golden Spiral and Aspect Ratios, in Lightroom's order; 76de46a) cycle with `O`, and `⇧O` turns the triangle and the spiral (eight ways), and `X` swaps the crop between portrait and landscape, in the Crop tool only. The Straighten tool (its button, or ⌘-drag in the crop) levels the photo along a drawn horizon or vertical. Lightroom's `crs:Crop*` fields map to Redlamp's crop (0e2aea7, `LightroomCrop`, checked against Redlamp's crop geometry for every orientation; applied once XMP sidecars import, EDT-12). Choose Overlays to Cycle and Choose Aspect Ratios (2a5b543): checkboxes in a popover beside the Overlay menu; `O` steps only through the chosen overlays, the Aspect Ratios overlay outlines only the chosen ratios, both remembered | [DT §7](darktable-findings.md#7-lenses-and-geometry) |
| LNS-07 | Our own line detector from the LSD paper | Build | P2 | M | DEC-05 | Accepted (2026-10-02): the owner asked for automatic Upright; to stay clear of DEC-05, the detector follows Burns, Hanson and Riseman's line-support regions (1986) and leaves out LSD's a-contrario validation | Done: `LineDetector` grows regions of pixels whose gradients agree within 22.5° on the 1024-pixel analysis image (EXIF-oriented, square-root luma, a light blur first) and fits each long, thin, dense region with its gradient-weighted principal axis; 50–420 ms per photo. It finds a turned rectangle's sides within 0.5° in noise and no long lines in noise alone. Stair-stepped (aliased) edges break into short runs, which real photos' soft edges don't | [DT §7](darktable-findings.md#7-lenses-and-geometry) |
| LNS-08 | Upright as a virtual camera rotation (Level, Vertical, Full, Guided all in late P2, deterministic Auto), mapping to Lightroom's Transform sliders; optional 4-corner rectangle mode | Do better | P2 | L | LNS-05, LNS-07 | Accepted (2026-10-01) | In progress: Guided Upright works. The Transform sliders are a virtual camera tilted, turned and rolled; drawing two to four guides solves the Vertical, Horizontal and Rotate that make them upright or level (a few Levenberg–Marquardt steps on their angles, damped so undetermined sliders stay put), and the guides stay on their edges in the photo's coordinates. Auto, Level, Vertical and Full now work from the detected edges (LNS-07): lines within 30° of vertical or level, a robust (Cauchy) fit over Rotate, Rotate and Vertical, or all three, and a correction only when the edges agree on it (most of their weight within 1.5° afterwards, spread over a quarter of the frame, inside the sliders' range), falling back Full to Vertical to Level to nothing. Auto halves perspective past 30 and eases it until Constrain to Image keeps 80% of the frame. A facade shot looking up (Vertical −25) is recovered within 2; on the six sample photos, none architectural, Auto levels by 0.1–1° and corrects only the product shot's verticals (5.2), where a first version without the agreement test warped all of them | [DT §7](darktable-findings.md#7-lenses-and-geometry) |
| LNS-09 | Chromatic aberration: profile and embedded TCA in the warp, automatic lateral CA, Lightroom-style Defringe | Adopt | P2 | M | LNS-01 | Accepted (2026-10-02): the owner asked for it | Done: a profile's own fringe correction (Sony's) applies with the profile; otherwise Remove Chromatic Aberration measures it, in 48 tiles at the finest pyramid level up to 3072 pixels, with a Lucas–Kanade step along the radius at edges across it and a fit of a·r + b·r³ per channel (17–57 ms per photo, once). It recovers a synthetic lens within 3·10⁻⁴ of its scale and cuts fringe misalignment by over 70%; on the Sony sample it agrees in sign and order with Sony's own tables, and on the Fujifilm one it removes visible red-blue fringes (about 6 pixels at the corners). Defringe greys purple or green (Lightroom's Amount and Hue ranges, as OKLab hue bands) within two texels of a stop or more of contrast, and leaves the same colours elsewhere | [DT §7](darktable-findings.md#7-lenses-and-geometry) |
| LNS-10 | Guided automatic defringe | Adopt | P3 | M | LNS-09 | Proposed | Not started | [DT §7](darktable-findings.md#7-lenses-and-geometry) |
| LNS-11 | The Lens Corrections panel names the matched lens profile and lists profiles that couldn't be read | Adopt | P2 | S | LNS-04 | Accepted (2026-10-03): the owner chose wave 1's follow-ups for agent wave 2a | Done (753c807): `LensCorrection.profileName` (not used in rendering) names a profile correction for its lens; the panel shows "Profile: ‹lens›" under Enable Profile Corrections and, when some profiles in the folder couldn't be read, how many, with files and reasons in its help | This tracker |
| LNS-12 | Custom crop aspect ratios, as Lightroom's Enter Custom…: a ratio (21 × 9) or a size in pixels (1080 × 1350) typed in and kept in the Aspect menu as a preset; and a button beside the menu that swaps the crop between portrait and landscape, as `X` does | Adopt | P2 | S | LNS-06 | Accepted (2026-10-08): the owner accepted it from #352 | Done (3f5efc9f): Enter Custom… at the end of the Aspect menu takes a ratio (21 × 9, 21:9, 2.39) or a size in pixels (1080 × 1350) and crops as typed, so 1080 × 1350 is a portrait crop of any photo; the five most recent stay in the menu for every editor, across launches, until Clear Custom Aspects. The presets keep the crop's orientation, and a button beside the lock swaps it, as `X` does. Original now keeps a photo shot in portrait portrait, at its own ratio: it gave a landscape crop | [#352](https://github.com/pdcgomes/redlamp/issues/352), sent from the app (2026-10-08): ratios typed in as a ratio or a resolution and saved as presets, and a way to switch the orientation |
| LNS-13 | Turn the photo by dragging outside the crop, as in Lightroom: outside the frame the pointer becomes a curved arrow, and the drag turns the photo as the Angle slider does (Constrain to Image included), with a fine grid while it turns; ⌘-drag and the Straighten tool still draw a level line | Adopt | P2 | S | LNS-06 | Accepted (2026-10-08): the owner accepted it from #353 | Done (ca22ada7): outside the crop the pointer is a curved arrow facing away from the crop's centre, and a drag turns the photo as far as the pointer goes round that centre, within Angle's ±45°, as the slider does: Constrain to Image fits the crop again, and the drag is one history step. | LNS-14 | Lens profiles across bodies: a profile of the photo's lens made on another body whose sensor is at least as large, preferring the photo's own body, then its make, then makes sharing its mount (L-mount: Leica, Panasonic and Sigma; Micro Four Thirds: OM System, Olympus and Panasonic); where the file names no lens, the lens ID from its maker note; camera names compared as LibRaw's canonical names. Adobe makes each lens's profile on one body or a few, and `LCPProfileLibrary.correction` uses it only on those, which leaves out most of the photographers LCP files serve: Canon, Nikon and Pentax users, whose raws carry no correction Redlamp reads, and adapted or third-party lenses. Existing edits whose Lens Profiles folder holds such a profile would render differently, so it comes with a new process version | Adopt | P2 | S | LNS-04 | Accepted (2026-10-14): the owner's choice, at first for 0.3.2's process version with CAM-29, DN-12 and LNS-02's Panasonic and OM System corrections; (2026-10-15) the owner moved it to process 17, in 0.3.3, with LNS-16, which fisheye profiles (LNS-17) join if they're ready | Done (commits "Lens profiles made on other bodies and in Camera Raw's folders, matched from process 17" and "Process 17: lens profiles made on other bodies and in Camera Raw's folders"): where every process from 5 finds no profile made on the photo's own body in Redlamp's folder, process 17 takes the photo's own body (by its file's names or LibRaw's canonical one), then another body of its make, then a make sharing its mount (L-mount: Leica, Panasonic, Sigma; Micro Four Thirds: Olympus and OM System, Panasonic), on a sensor at least as large within a tenth, its models scaled to the photo's frame by the two crop factors (formats within 4% taken for one); the lens by its name, the profile's display name or the lens's `AlternateLensNames`, and where the file names none by Nikon's LensIDNumber where the maker's profiles carrying it and agreeing with the lens's focal lengths describe one lens. Each raw now carries its crop factor (the stated 35 mm-equivalent focal length, rounded to the format LibRaw knows, else LibRaw's format, overruled by the EXIF focal plane resolution where that says full frame, since LibRaw takes Canon bodies it doesn't know, such as the EOS R6 Mark III, for APS-C), LibRaw's canonical name, Nikon's lens number and the lens's focal lengths. Wherever process 16 finds a profile, process 17 finds the same one, so a photo has one correction, tagged with the first process that applies it; makes outside the three tiers aren't matched, an adapted lens being LNS-15's Custom choice. Process 17's references are process 16's, byte for byte, on both GPUs | [RMK §9.3](rawmakase-findings.md#93-lens-profiles) |
| LNS-15 | Choosing a lens profile by hand, as Lightroom's Lens Corrections panel does: its Setup, Make, Model and Profile menus, listing the profiles on the Mac that cover the photo's sensor, for an adapted or manual lens or one the file doesn't name; the choice stored by name and file name, as `crs:LensProfileName` and `LensProfileFilename` store it, so Lightroom edits brought across keep theirs, and a named profile that isn't on the Mac reported with what stands in | Adopt | P2 | M | LNS-14 | Accepted (2026-10-14): the owner's choice, aimed at 0.3.3 | Done (commit "Lens profiles chosen by hand: Lightroom's Setup, Make, Model and Profile menus"): the Lens Corrections panel's Setup, Make, Model and Profile menus list every folder's raw profiles made on the photo's own body or on a sensor at least as large within a tenth (its own body's alone without its crop factor); the edit keeps the choice in the sidecar's `lensProfile` (Setup, name and file name), which `LensProfileChoice` maps from Lightroom's `crs:LensProfileSetup`, `LensProfileName` and `LensProfileFilename` for EDT-23's converter. Every process from 5 still matches rectilinear profiles alone, as LNS-04 did; process 17's wider match takes fisheye profiles too, and tags the correction process 17 (`LCPProfileLibrary.correction`), and the Profile menu (LNS-15) lists them. Step 2 is built (65fcfa431, 678af287b): every folder Redlamp remembers keeps a security-scoped bookmark while the app is unsandboxed, as the sandboxed app needs to reach it without asking: the working set's, made before a folder is saved and resolved before its path, the library's roots, the import's destination and backup, export presets' folders and Export with Previous's, with earlier versions' made at launch; the import's destination is unset until the first import asks for it; the Lightroom import asks for each root it can't read with Allow Access…; and `Folders.plist` in Redlamp's folder copies the settings that name folders, which a sandboxed launch takes what its settings lack from. Building it found that a process in a sandbox of another kind, as agents' shells are, can't make security-scoped bookmarks, so those are plain there (the study). Left for step 5: the staging sweep inside the destination's access. Next, step 3: document types and the open event, and a photo opened on its own keeping its edit on this Mac until its folder is granted.
