| EXT-04 | Photos: a Photo Editing Extension that edits a Photos photo with Redlamp's engine and its recipes, saving its adjustment data so the edit stays editable; Photos' Favorite paired with Redlamp's Pick flag on every macOS version Redlamp supports (`PHAsset.isFavorite`, set with `PHAssetChangeRequest.isFavorite`), so a favourite in Photos is a Pick in Redlamp and a Pick written back is a favourite; and star ratings read from Photos and written back on macOS 27 and later, where PhotoKit gives a photo a rating of none or one to five stars (`PHAsset.rating`, set with `PHAssetChangeRequest.rating`). A rating doesn't carry to Favorite: before macOS 27, where Photos keeps no star rating, it stays in Redlamp. Left for the build: what becomes of a Reject, which has no counterpart in Photos (whether rejecting a favourite un-favourites it); what un-favouriting a photo in Photos does to its Pick (whether the Pick is cleared); and which wins when the flag and the favourite have both changed since they were last in step | Build | P2 | L | LIB-57 | Proposed; photos opened from the Photos library moved to LIB-57 (2026-10-27); the owner added Photos' star ratings from #394 (2026-10-22); the owner paired Photos' Favorite with Redlamp's Pick flag on every macOS version, so star ratings no longer map to Favorite (2026-10-26) | Not started: PhotoKit's rating needs the macOS 27 SDK, from Xcode 27, and this Mac and CI build with Xcode 26 | [Lightroom comparison](../lightroom-comparison.md); [#394](https://github.com/pdcgomes/redlamp/issues/394), sent from the app (2026-10-13): albums opened from Photos, edits written back to it, and its star ratings read and written; [`PHAsset.rating`](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/photos/phasset/rating-swift.property) in Apple's PhotoKit documentation, macOS 27 and later |
| EXT-05 | Save to Photos: a photo imported from Apple Photos (LIB-54) gives its Redlamp edit back to its Photos original, rendered at full size with a compact copy of the edit as its adjustment data, as DEC-76 chose for the iPhone, so Photos and the photographer's other devices show it and Photos' Revert to Original takes it off; on demand, over the selected photos, with one permission prompt per batch | Build | P4 | M | LIB-54, DEC-76 | Proposed (2026-10-26): the owner chose it as a later row, after 0.3.5's import, which only reads Photos (DEC-106) | Not started | The owner (2026-10-26) |
| AUD-01 | Data safety fixes from audit round 1: exports never replace a photo, in the app or the CLI; a sidecar this build can't read, or couldn't write back without loss, is never deleted or overwritten; emptying a sidecar keeps its history; unknown fields survive at every level; iCloud conflicts resolve only the copies that were merged; failed saves are reported, pending saves flushed on quit, and another writer's changes kept; unchanged sidecars aren't rewritten; interrupted saves and deletes are cleaned up; iCloud merges keep every copy's rating, flag and label; a damaged edit has a way out; a mask shape with several kinds opens read-only (DATA-01 to DATA-15) | Build | P2 | L | — | Accepted (2026-10-03) | Done (b74fafe, d3f9016, 9421806, e02e3ef, 7ae1244, 8786f24): landed in b74fafe: exports never replace a photo, in the app or the CLI; sidecars that can't be read, or that a save would change, open read-only and are never overwritten or deleted; unknown fields kept at every level; emptied sidecars keep their history; iCloud conflicts merged only when every copy can be (DATA-01, 03, 04, 06, 08, 12). Landed in d3f9016: one ordered save queue with a 2 s maximum wait; failed saves shown with Retry, kept per photo and retried; saving before quitting; another writer's changes merged, the other edit kept as a snapshot (DATA-02, 05, 07, 09). Landed in 9421806: damaged gain maps, profile gain table maps and JPEG XL sizes, and any decode-service reply the app can't use, fail that photo instead of crashing or over-allocating (FUZZ-01 to 03, in the decoder and the reply); iCloud merges keep every copy's rating, flag and label (DATA-13); a mask shape with several kinds opens read-only (DATA-15); interrupted saves and deletes leave nothing behind (DATA-11); unchanged sidecars aren't rewritten, already on main (DATA-10). Landed in e02e3ef: focus-stack frames decode through the decoder service like photos, and the app never decodes in its own process (a service that can't start fails the photo); a stack frame that can't be read is left out and can't be a brush's source, the stack's info says how many couldn't be read, and such a stack is merged once and kept until one of them can be read, rather than on every open, export and stroke; a raw whose preview's offset and length overflow is refused instead of crashing the app (FUZZ-01, FUZZ-02). Landed in 7ae1244: a rating, flag or label, Auto Sync, pasting onto a selection, Update AI Masks and Heal Dust never write over a sidecar that couldn't be read; a read that fails for now is retried like other failed saves, an edit that doesn't decode is reported, a batch skips and reports the photo, and Undo Sync says which photos it couldn't put back (DATA-18 outside the editor, under DATA-01). Landed in 8786f24: a photo whose sidecar can't be read opens read-only and is read again until it can be, never unedited, and work started meanwhile can't land on its edit (DATA-18); a damaged edit is called damaged, and Start Over keeps it in the sidecar beside a new edit (DATA-14); a save that keeps failing keeps every visit's history and shows another writer's edit merged. What the app still parses itself is DATA-17, waiting for review | Audit round 1 (2 October 2026, at 32774fc), Data safety plan |
| AUD-02 | Responsiveness fixes from audit round 1: drawing or selecting a mask no longer rebuilds the whole Masking panel; a photo's embedded mattes are read once per session, off the main thread; switching to an already decoded photo updates the UI once, without blocking the main thread (RESP-02 to RESP-04) | Build | P2 | M | AUD-01 | Accepted (2026-10-03) | Done (1cdf47c, 65bd110, 4a88275): the Masking panel keeps its rows (drawing and selecting still cost 50–87 ms, RESP-13); embedded mattes are read once per photo, off the main thread; switching to a photo decoded ahead changes the editor once, when its edit has been read, so its first frame comes in about 40 ms instead of 125–140 ms, with mostly 20–30 ms of main-thread time instead of 180–195 ms (measured at a load average of about 50). While that edit is read, the photo left takes no edits, ratings, flags, labels and Previous and Next go to the photo being opened, and going straight back starts a new visit; past 100 ms the new photo's thumbnail shows, as before (RESP-02 to RESP-04) | Audit round 1 (2 October 2026, at 32774fc), Responsiveness plan |
| AUD-03 | Memory fixes from audit round 1: the detail stage's scratch textures and idle cached outputs are purgeable between renders, and its working set is sized to the current photo instead of the largest view ever rendered (MEM-01) | Build | P2 | M | — | Accepted (2026-10-03) | Done (ec098fd; 5785c16 keeps a new command buffer at a freed one's address from being taken for it): the detail stage holds 1.3 GB instead of 3.1 GB at 24 MP 1:1 and 2.3 GB instead of 7.5 GB at 60 MP, keeps nothing when idle, and tiles large views within its budget; output unchanged | Audit round 1 (2 October 2026, at 32774fc), Memory plan |
| AUD-04 | Concurrency fixes from audit round 1: Auto Tone, Upright, Auto White Balance, the eyedropper and Apply Recipe apply their result only to the photo they were computed for; exporting another photo no longer evicts the open photo's mask data (CONC-03, CONC-04) | Build | P2 | S | AUD-01 | Accepted (2026-10-03) | Done (0d0ef26, d5eada3, b3538ee): exporting another photo no longer evicts the open photo's mask data (masked frames during an export 2.4 ms instead of 62 ms; kept rasters capped at 128 MB a photo and 256 MB in all, purgeable); analysis results land only on the photo they were computed for, at all 25 places that apply one, and Remove Dust across a selection heals the open photo in the editor (CONC-03, CONC-04) | Audit round 1 (2 October 2026, at 32774fc), Concurrency plan |
| AUD-05 | Render pipeline fixes from audit round 1: slider and brush drags render only the visible area; the noise-reduction pass is cached on its own, so Sharpening, Texture and Clarity drags don't recompute it (PIPE-01, PIPE-03) | Build | P2 | M | AUD-03 | Accepted (2026-10-03) | Done (0123344, a6fff29): landed in 0123344: before process 11, Sharpening, Texture and Clarity drags keep the noise-reduced image instead of denoising again (about 13–17 ms to 5–7 ms a frame), a Noise drag reuses the image it replaces, and from any process a mask's Texture, Clarity or Sharpness drag no longer reruns noise reduction; output unchanged (PIPE-03). Also fixed: from process 11, with Luminance at 0, adding a mask drew interactive frames from the detail ladder computed without it (PIPE-13). Landed in a6fff29: while a slider, palette or scroll edit is in progress at zoom, only the visible area plus a 64-pixel guard is rendered, and the margin once it ends, so a Luminance drag at 1:1 takes about 30 ms a frame instead of 94 and a Sharpen drag 9 instead of 28 (an 1800 × 1100 window, at a load average of about 72); a pan reuses the overview and histogram; output unchanged (PIPE-01). PIPE-16, found on the way, waits for the owner | Audit round 1 (2 October 2026, at 32774fc), Render pipeline plan |
| AUD-06 | Data safety fixes from audit round 1, second batch: a GPU buffer that can't be allocated partway through encoding never leaves an encoder open (DATA-16); the files the app still parses itself move behind the decode service in six steps, each within 10% of main: the cheaper readers first, then thumbnails with the service started at launch (DATA-17) | Build | P2 | M | AUD-01 | Accepted (2026-10-06) | In progress: landed in 3322440: AUD-01's save tests wait on their conditions with a 30 s limit, so load can't fail them; in 95c9a8e, cf8b990 and 0fb6082: a compute encoder ends however its work exits, so a buffer or texture that can't be allocated partway through the session build, develop, the detail passes or retouch throws an error instead of crashing a Debug build, and the purity check fails on a throw while an encoder is open (DATA-16); in f883659 to 1f66424: focus-stack detection reads each photo's capture settings and its small thumbnail in the decode service, a folder's captures in calls of at most 1,000 files, so a damaged or hostile file found while looking for stacks can crash only the service (DATA-17, step 3 of 7); in 43519ca: a Debug build stops on any call to the decode service from the main thread, where a service stuck on a hostile file would hang the app; in 0723b81 and 6483937: export reads its source's metadata, and whether a file in the folder is an earlier export, in the decode service, and the Export dialog works out its Saves-as name off the main thread, so its longest stall with the service cold is 1.5 ms instead of 541 (DATA-17, step 4); in f7e2b4c and b40c6b8: an imported HaldCLUT image is decoded in the decode service and read off the main thread, giving the same look table at every level and bit depth, so a level-16 import stalls the main thread 1 to 14 ms instead of 0.7 to 1.1 s and the app's peak is 585 MB instead of 877 (DATA-17, step 5); in 13e088b and 6c4de69, built by Concurrency: Camera Bench identifies files and reads their camera previews in the decode service, which sends each preview back as pixels, and in 08fc3f8 and 7aa3b84 a preview in a colour space without a name comes back drawn in Display P3, so the app parses no ICC profile from the service and refuses a reply that carries one (DATA-17, step 6); in d26298c: a HaldCLUT image larger than a 65-point table is refused from its size before it's decoded, where levels 12 and 16 were refused only after a full decode; in 769e8da and 2449953: a photo's embedded mattes, such as an iPhone's sky, hair and depth, are found and read in the decode service through the engine's decoder, giving the same masks, and the purity check fails on a direct read; opening a photo stays within 10% (the NEF 762 to 814 ms, the DNG and HEIC unchanged) and a Sky mask within 4% (DATA-17, step 2); in 7ae616f: a call to the decode service from the main thread stops before it reads the file, so one whose file can't be read no longer gets past the guard, as CI's run showed; in b5666dd, 53790ee and f38db73: the Export dialog's and Export with Previous's tests wait for their reads instead of a delay CI's runner outlasted, create the application themselves, and open no Finder window (DATA-17, steps 4 and the guard). Next: DATA-17 step 7, the filmstrip's thumbnails, once the owner's M5 kit run gives its baseline | Audit round 1 (2 October 2026, at 32774fc), Data safety plan; findings approved 6 October 2026 |
| AUD-07 | Responsiveness fixes from audit round 1, second batch: hosted controls redraw only for the settings they show (RESP-01); the histogram no longer redraws on the main thread 30 times a second (RESP-05); Base Look tables are decoded off the main thread, and only when they change (RESP-06, LEAN-05); pans and zooms don't re-run the canvas area (RESP-08); a run of nudges is one history step (RESP-09); the performance tooling measures what the README quotes, each run keeps its own report, and a kit runs the measurements on a second Mac (RESP-10, RESP-16); idle canvases stop waking (RESP-12); selecting a mask doesn't rebuild its editor (RESP-13); Undo Sync, Paste from Previous and Remove Dust's selection read and write off the main thread (RESP-14); the mask model catalogue loads off the main thread (RESP-15); frames reach the canvas without waiting on the main thread, if a probe still finds the wait once the fixes above land (RESP-07); and a build that counts the Tone Curve's draws finds the stall's trigger before it is fixed (RESP-11) | Build | P2 | L | AUD-02 | Accepted (2026-10-06) | In progress: landed in 8055d73 and 0429dae: each performance run writes its own reports, so runs from other sessions can't delete or take them, a sweep opens every panel by default and reports each frame's time from request to frame (RESP-10, RESP-16); and a draw-counting build for RESP-11's trigger, for the owner to drag between screens; in 096c21f: each run's launch arguments sit beside its own bundle (RESP-16); in 9228f96 and 56bf9b9: Undo Sync, Paste from Previous and Remove Dust read and write sidecars off the main thread, and Undo Sync runs as a batch of its own with its progress shown (RESP-14); in a5d3f51, 5b5987f and 4afade3: a kit runs perf-record.sh's measurements and the decode service's figures on another Mac with only Terminal, and its results are imported as that Mac's series (RESP-10); in 0860685 and 67cabca: the histogram's channels are drawn into an image off the main thread, and what's around them only when it changes, so a new histogram no longer draws on the main thread, about 2.6 ms of it 30 times a second while a photo renders, and it looks the same (RESP-05); in 67f4513 and cf156bd: a run of = or - presses on a slider is one history step, recorded 400 ms after the last press, before another shortcut or Undo, or as another photo is selected, so holding = makes one Undo step and no longer reloads the History list or the menu bar with each press (RESP-09); in 28e8c1e: a test that another writer's edit adopted while the next photo is read is kept on going back, which passes as main stands (RESP-04); in ad923c4 and 9630176: an idle canvas, the editor's or the Navigator's, takes its display link off its render thread's run loop and puts it back for the next scene, so the thread sleeps instead of waking 5 to 6 times a second (RESP-12), and in 0b065e6 scripts/idle-trace.sh counts those wakeups, from the owner's Terminal since the sandbox blocks xctrace; in 5f9ef22 and 3f56458: opening a photo asks for the AI mask list off the main actor and keeps the answer only for the photo still open, so the open's longest main-actor wait is 6 to 10 ms instead of up to 348 ms with the catalogue cold, and the 2 s stall the smoke tier caught can't recur, and in 45928f8 the mask-editing tests and in 632003d the Even Skin Tone test wait for the list rather than a second (RESP-15); in 38e94c3, with Lean code's LEAN-05: Base Looks register only what changed, upload a table on first use outside the lock, and register at launch in the background, so toggling a favourite registers none of the 150 it did and a library change no longer discards kept overviews, and in d8b845b each look registers with a loader and is read on its first render, a look that can't be read showing as missing, so launch registration costs 0 to 1 ms of CPU and 0.1 MB instead of 80 to 116 ms and about 50 MB (RESP-06, with LEAN-05); in 32a93ff and c60eaa3: the inspector's hosted controls observe their own value and the process version instead of the whole edit, and the Base Look groups are sorted once per catalogue change, so an Exposure drag with every panel open costs 0.7 ms of main-thread time a tick instead of 8.2 (RESP-01); in 204ec3a and 87afdc5: pans and zooms redraw the canvas from its own view, once per main-actor turn, without re-running the canvas area's SwiftUI body, so a pan at 1:1 in the Edit tool costs about 0.38 ms of main-thread time an event instead of 0.75 (RESP-08); in 124e825 and 1d941c0: the inspector column sizes its document once a layout pass and keeps its rows' heights, where six rows growing together were each measured 25 times, so closing a panel takes 3.8 ms instead of 5.4; selecting a mask stays near 60 ms, the old masking panel's rows rejoining the window, which the new Masks panel avoids (RESP-13). RESP-07's probe: during a drag a frame waits 0.12 ms at p50 and 1.3 to 1.9 ms at p95 for the main thread, so the presenter it proposed would save little and isn't built; each frame reaches the screen 49.9 ms after it's drawn, three refreshes at 60 Hz and 90% of the time from engine to screen, which the owner may make a finding of its own; in a93381e and 5413f0e: a People picker opened before the list of AI masks arrives works out which parts need a model once it does, so ticking Hair asks for SAM 3 again, which fetching the list after the open had broken, as CI's runs showed (RESP-15). Next: the owner's call on both | Audit round 1 (2 October 2026, at 32774fc), Responsiveness plan; findings approved 6 October 2026 |
| AUD-08 | Memory fixes from audit round 1, second batch: at 1:1 the area rendered around the view is capped at rest (MEM-02); closed photos are released (MEM-03); the session cache counts all of a photo's memory and answers memory pressure (MEM-04); opening a photo copies its sensor data once (MEM-05); canvases let go of surfaces the engine dropped (MEM-06); AI models load once and unload when idle (MEM-07); a focus-stack merge keeps only what it still needs (MEM-08); closing the editor window releases its memory (MEM-09); and a failed GPU buffer can't leave the retouch cache pointing at an unretouched photo (MEM-10) | Build | P2 | L | AUD-03 | Accepted (2026-10-06) | In progress: landed in a1d7058, 3d0dc28 and 0dc07b1: a render that fails after its healing or removal spots were encoded no longer leaves the photo cached without them, since every render error path runs one shared rollback of what each stage recorded, and readbacks roll back too (MEM-10). In afeb7c3 and 1dcccf7: a canvas keeps only its last two frames' surfaces, so a 1:1 frame's are freed on the next frame (MEM-06); in 418b7da and 6f5d9ee: at rest at 1:1 the render covers 2.25 times the view, not the whole photo, saving 571 MB of GPU memory at 24 MP (MEM-02); in cc3bc93 and e36dddc: the session cache counts everything a photo holds, not only its pyramid, keeps the photos it prefetches within its budget, least wanted first, and lets go of cached photos when macOS runs short of memory, so a 300 MB budget keeps one 24 MP photo where four held 1,196 MB (MEM-04); in 07a05af and febd3f0: a focus-stack merge keeps its colour copy packed, drops each frame's full-size luma once it is aligned, and makes no extra fused copies (MEM-08); in c4361b7 and 4e4fcd8: the analysis copy is packed as RGB, 12 bytes a pixel, with the same floats, so every result that reads it is unchanged and a photo holds 5 to 12 MB less (MEM-04); in 0b19b3d and d14ce27: a decode copies the service's reply once and bitmaps draw straight into their samples, so a 155 MB Panasonic G9 raw holds 155 MB in each process instead of about 310, with the same samples (MEM-05); in d79fcfb and 33b81d2: opening another photo releases what the engine keeps for the others, its AI analysis, mattes, detail stage, retouches, fills and comparison, and a memory warning empties the detail stage, so after leaving a 24 MP photo the engine holds 589 MB of GPU memory instead of 1,208 and the photo is freed (MEM-03); in 5f3ff6c and 46c6ceb: closing the editor window releases every photo, cached session, mask and retouch the engine holds (MEM-09); in 8830818 and 408114f: the AI mask models load once, however many ask at the same time, and unload after 5 minutes unused, when the Masking tool closes, on a memory warning and when the window closes, and warm-up stops with the tool, so closing the tool takes the footprint from 2.85 GB over baseline to about 0.5 GB (MEM-07); in 221e0ad and da71067: a still rendered for a photo no longer open, such as one the Masking tool's warm-up finishes after the window has closed, or a focus stack's preview, keeps nothing in the detail stage and leaves the open photo's detail alone, where it kept 459 MB of textures (MEM-09). The 0.5 GB left after the Masking tool closes is the system allocator's, freed memory it keeps for reuse: with its large-allocation cache off (MallocLargeCache=0) the footprint is 40 MB after each release and the loaded peak 1.4 GB instead of 2.1, and the engine's own caches hold 13 MB by then. MEM-09's check in the app: after the window closes it holds 205 MB more than at launch (124 MB with that cache off), or 655 MB after the Masking tool was used, against a target of 50 MB; in 2389821 and fe5ea3a: an AI mask's analysis or matte render, or a model's result, that finishes after its photo was let go of is returned to its caller but not kept, so closing the window mid-render frees the photo's session (311 MB for the test's NEF) and 64 MB of renders and embedding (MEM-09); in 75a6091: the idle-unload test allows seconds instead of 50 ms, which CI's runner overslept (MEM-07); in 1afaee6 to 4d74cf8: a still or a dust search of a photo that isn't open, or a still that finishes after the window closed, keeps none of that photo's retouches or masks and evicts none of the open photo's, and the shared embedding cache lets go of its copy in memory when the models unload or the window closes, so after two exports of another photo the engine no longer keeps its session (311 MB) or its two retouched copies (537 MB), and mask textures come to the open photo's 199 MB instead of 466 MB (MEM-09). Next: the owner's call on the allocator setting and on MEM-09's 50 MB target | Audit round 1 (2 October 2026, at 32774fc), Memory plan; findings approved 6 October 2026 |
| AUD-09 | Concurrency fixes from audit round 1, second batch: going to a photo that hasn't decoded and straight back keeps the engine on the photo you came back to (CONC-01); a failed render can't leave mask caches pointing at masks it never drew (CONC-05); Paste and Update AI Masks across a selection apply the open photo's change in the editor, as Remove Dust does (CONC-06); and the sky refinement keeps to the photo it started on (CONC-07) | Build | P2 | M | AUD-04 | Accepted (2026-10-06) | Done: going to a photo that hasn't decoded and straight back keeps the engine on the photo you came back to (CONC-01, 2bf435c); a failed or dropped render forgets the masks it drew and the caches it grew (CONC-05, e6fd11e); Sync, Paste, Update AI Masks, Auto Sync and Undo Sync change the photo open in the editor as a step its Undo takes back, and re-check each photo as they save it (CONC-06, 3b6063a); and a Sky mask's edges are solved on the photo it was computed for (CONC-07, 10bda68) | Audit round 1 (2 October 2026, at 32774fc), Concurrency plan; findings approved 6 October 2026 |

In 0c753d2 and e77e0f0: switching a focus stack's merge method reuses its frames' alignment and depth, with a bit-identical result (PIPE-10); in dcc1639, 01056dc and 88cc58b: Background made after Subject on the same photo takes the Subject's matte, its exact inverse, instead of solving its edges again, so it comes back in milliseconds instead of 5 to 6 s, byte for byte the same, with the matte kept on the engine (PIPE-09); in 0249f27 and eebc1ba: when the detail stage moves to another photo it lets go of the last one's cached outputs, sharpening analyses, ladder, kept sources and Clarity bases, and its session, 150 MB of textures and a 247 MB pyramid at 24 MP, with renders unchanged (PIPE-14); in 0ea6e22 and 7c17b3c: a frame whose size changes renders into a surface large enough for it instead of making three new ones, so frames of alternating sizes take 6.6 ms against 18.0 and a pinch's slowest frames 15.7 ms against 21.5, with the same pixels (PIPE-05); in b3aa7d4 and 421f11a: the detail stage rounds a cached render's work area out to a 256-texel grid and serves any later area it covers, so a crop Angle drag at Fit reuses it on 39 of 40 ticks, each under 0.1 ms instead of 39 ms, with exports unchanged and previews within the 1e-3 already allowed between a region and the whole frame (PIPE-02); in da59540 and 143537b: the detail stage keeps the work textures its caches let go of, parked as purgeable within its scratch budget, and writes over them instead of making new ones, so once warm a Luminance drag at Fit makes no textures where it made 200 (7.9 GB over 40 ticks), a 1:1 Before/After pan none of its 100 and a still's tiles none of their 36, with renders unchanged (PIPE-04); the matte, Depth Anything 3 and SAM 3 run when a mask needs them, so a photo's first Sky, Depth, Landscape or People-parts mask pays for them then, with masks and renders unchanged (PIPE-08) | Audit round 1 (2 October 2026, at 32774fc), Render pipeline plan; findings approved 6 October 2026, PIPE-18 on 7 October |
| AUD-11 | Lean code fixes from audit round 1: the harness builds in Release (LEAN-01); release binaries are stripped (LEAN-02); LibRaw exports only what Redlamp calls (LEAN-03); the decode service shares the app's frameworks instead of carrying copies (LEAN-04); Base Look tables ship as compressed binary, read back identically (LEAN-06); the Recipe Lab and Charts leave the shipping app (LEAN-07); the slowest type-checks are fixed (LEAN-08); unused and test-only code leaves the frameworks (LEAN-09); colour math is shared rather than copied (LEAN-10); the CLI uses RedlampDocument's sidecar layout (LEAN-11); the release ships only Apple Silicon code (LEAN-12) | Build | P2 | L | — | Accepted (2026-10-06) | Done: in 142b226 and f003cca: the harness builds in Release again, its scenes drawing their mask through the path a drag takes, and CI builds it so (LEAN-01); in 2dee6b0: the four slowest type-checks are fixed, the style vector from 5 s to 4 ms and a mask handle from 2 s to 16 ms, with none left over 1 s, and in 5f62e75 a framework's Debug build fails on a body over 1.5 s or an expression over 1 s (LEAN-08); in dba3546: the tests rank the palette's real slider rows and the unused ranker is gone, and in fe89ee3 to c46c930 unused declarations leave seven packages, 283 lines (LEAN-09). In 173390a and 664ed3d: the Recipe Lab moves into RedlampLab, which only the harness links, so neither it nor Charts ships in the app (LEAN-07). In 58d4785 and 490e48b: the packages build for Apple Silicon only, and the release thins Sparkle to arm64 and fails if any Mach-O in the bundle holds another architecture, so all 24 are arm64 and the bundle goes from 112,048 to 84,592 KB; Sparkle's update from an older copy is checked on 0.2.6's candidate by the release's test-update.sh (LEAN-12); in 2b7c98d and fc252a5: what ships is stripped of all but its exported symbols, and each release's dSYMs go up beside it as dSYMs-<version>.zip, so the bundle goes from 84,600 to 56,372 KB, and the release fails if a binary still carries debugging symbols or has no dSYM, or if the bundle outgrows a budget 2% above that (LEAN-02), and in 1ab29f8 its failure says how to raise the budget; in 76f7c38 and 61243b3: LibRaw is built with hidden visibility, so RedlampServices exports none of it and dead-stripping drops what Redlamp doesn't call, 512 KB from each of its two copies (the bundle 55,372 KB), with every decode and reference unchanged, and the release fails if RedlampServices exports LibRaw again (LEAN-03); in 1ed2e5a and 9969777: the decode service loads the app's frameworks instead of its own copies, so it goes from 2,304 to 80 KB and the bundle from 55,480 to 53,256 KB, starting and decoding when signed by the release's team with the hardened runtime, and the release fails if an XPC service carries its own copy of the app's frameworks (LEAN-04); the release room's dry run checks the Developer ID signature, notarization and the black-box tier on 0.2.6's candidate. In 28bbd6f to 348cc92: the sRGB transfer functions, the Rec.2020 and Rec.709 luma weights and OKLab have one Swift copy, in RedlampColor, which Recipes, the sky masks, the engine and Point Color's swatch use, with every value bit for bit as before (TransferFunctionTests pins them against the old expressions and Develop.metal), and the purity check fails on a new copy outside it; removal's two copies wait for the removal work (LEAN-10). In d95b32c and a02fed7: the last unused declarations on LEAN-09's list go, DNGNoiseProfile's read from a URL and RunStore's update and comparisons (LEAN-09). In 33fa0be and 35900fb: the CLI reads a `--recipe` sidecar through SidecarStore, as the app does, with file coordination, and reports a damaged one instead of rendering it unedited; a package sidecar with a mask, which the CLI used to render as if unedited, now renders its edit (LEAN-11). LEAN-05 was merged into RESP-06 (AUD-07). In e0db4ef and 3ae48ea: the 69 bundled Base Look tables ship as LZFSE-compressed JSON, each decompressing to its original bytes, with an index of their details that launch reads instead of the tables, so the app goes from 53,864 to 43,116 KB and building the recipe library on the main thread from about 200 ms to 20 to 63 ms warm, and the release's size budget is 44,000 KB (LEAN-06). | Audit round 1 (2 October 2026, at 32774fc), Lean code plan; findings approved 6 October 2026 |

## 9. AI denoise and focus stacking (product priorities)

| ID | Item | Recommended | Phase | Size | Depends on | Decision | Status | Source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| DN-01 | Noise profiles: DNG NoiseProfile tag, calibrated per-camera profiles, blind estimation; calibration tool and the first 10 bodies | Build | P2 | 5 ew | — | Accepted (2026-09-30) | In progress: the DNG NoiseProfile tag is read, otherwise noise is measured blind at open (same-colour sample differences in 16 px tiles, the 5th percentile per brightness, a weighted Poisson–Gaussian fit; about 30 ms). Busy scenes in lossy-compressed raws still read up to about 3× too noisy (see DN-10). The calibrated profiles and their tool are done (05a8cdd): `redlamp noise calibrate` fits each ISO by photon transfer from pairs of frames shot alike (each pair's difference cancels the scene and fixed-pattern noise; a weighted line per colour through 64 px tiles), profiles ship in RedlampServices' resources and are interpolated across ISO on log scales, at most a stop past the calibrated ones, and the cascade is the DNG tag, then the camera's profile, then the measurement, which wins when a raw is far cleaner than its profile (noise reduction in camera). `redlamp noise check` shows which applies. The first 10 bodies wait on calibration frames (DN-05) | [AI §2.2](ai-findings.md#22-noise-profiles-and-calibration), [DT §3](darktable-findings.md#3-cameras-and-raw-data) |
| DN-02 | Classical NR v1: luma pyramid shrinkage, luma-guided chroma, HQ non-local means pass, Lightroom Detail-panel mapping | Build | P2 | 8–11 ew | DN-01, DEC-05 | Accepted (2026-09-30) | Done (commit "Non-local means for luma at 1:1 and in exports"): v1 renders behind the Detail panel's six noise sliders. Five-scale à-trous B3 shrinkage (non-negative garrote, chroma shrunk as a vector) in a noise-stabilised (generalised Anscombe) opponent space, with each scale's noise measured through the real demosaic and pyramid. Dragging a noise slider at 1:1 renders in 9–10 ms (p95 14 ms). Chroma follows luma edges (Lee's sigma filter, 1983, across channels: a neighbour's chroma counts as much as its luma resembles the centre's within three of the level's noise sigmas), so colour no longer bleeds across them: around a clipped orange light on a blue ground at Color 100, the colour cast falls by 90% (0.0284 to 0.0029 at 4x noise), with PSNR, texture and flat chroma unchanged. Leaving clipped samples out of the filter (Foi 2009) was tried and dropped: here noise reduction runs after highlight reconstruction, so clipped samples are already plausible, and excluding them made a coloured light's cast slightly worse. Luma keeps more detail by shrinking each coefficient by its 3×3 neighbourhood's energy at that scale rather than by its own size (Lee's local statistics, 1980), so faint texture beside real texture survives: Luminance 50 now gains 2.2 dB at 2x noise and 3.0 dB at 4x (from 1.0 and 1.65), about 1 dB more than before at the same texture retained. The extra pass costs about 1 ms of GPU time on a 2560 × 1600 view at 1:1 and runs only when luma is being reduced; Color alone and sharpening's separator (which keeps the plain garrote it was calibrated with) cost what they did. The HQ pass is in: at 1:1 and in exports (pyramid level 0) with Luminance on, luma becomes non-local means (DEC-21) of the noisy texels, weighted by how alike the wavelet result's 3×3 patches are within 7×7. Guiding by the wavelet result rather than the noisy image is what makes it work: Luminance 50 now gains 3.0 dB at 2x noise and 4.1 dB at 4x, keeping 90% and 72% of the texture (the wavelet alone: 2.2 and 3.0 dB, 85% and 61%), and Luminance 100 keeps 81% where it kept 70%. 5×5 patches, 11×11 windows and keeping some of the wavelet's luma were all worse or no better. Tiled in threadgroup memory with a 2×4 block of texels per thread, it adds about 1.3 ms of GPU time to a 2560 × 1600 view at 1:1 (5.5 ms in its first, per-texel form). v1 uses only techniques published before 1996, so DEC-05 gates only those later pieces (non-local means is cleared by DEC-21). A benchmark (`NoiseBenchmarkTests`: dead leaves, a flat patch and a clipped highlight with exact Poisson–Gaussian noise and a matching profile, scored against the noise-free pipeline) gives the baseline: Luminance 50 gains 1.0 dB at 2x noise and 1.65 dB at 4x, keeping 86% and 62% of the texture; Color 25 cuts flat chroma error by 60% | [AI §2.1](ai-findings.md#21-classical-denoiser-nr-v1) |
| DN-03 | Sensor cleanup stage: hot pixels, banding, color bias | Build | P2 | 2 ew | DN-01 | Proposed | In progress: hot pixels are repaired before demosaicing (8 noise sigmas and 2× above every neighbour within two pixels, other colours included); no false repairs on the six sample cameras. Banding: row and column offsets are measured in the optical-black margins (LibRaw's left and top margins, used only when at least 16 photosites wide and at the black level), with outliers clipped at 5 sigmas and each line's estimate shrunk by the share of its variance that exceeds noise, and subtracted on the GPU with the black level. Of the sample cameras only the Canon R6 has masked margins; at ISO 100 its rows band by 0.17 DN RMS (1.3 DN pixel noise), and that is corrected; the Fujifilm's top rows and the Sony's extra columns aren't at the black level, so are left alone. Colour bias not started | [AI §2.1](ai-findings.md#21-classical-denoiser-nr-v1) |
| DN-04 | Core ML timing on an A17 Pro iPhone for NAFNet raw variants and SAM 2.1 | Adopt | P5 | S | — | Proposed | Not started. | DN-11 | Study: what Lightroom's raw Denoise is and what is published about it, the research on demosaicing and joint demosaicing and denoising, and a measured comparison of Redlamp, a pre-demosaic prototype and open models on synthetic scenes with exact truth and real RawNIND pairs | Build | P2 | M | — | Accepted (2026-10-06): the owner asked for it, measured without Lightroom outputs | Done (commit "Raw denoise study: Lightroom's Denoise, joint demosaicing and Redlamp (DN-11)"): Lightroom's Denoise is one network that demosaics and denoises the mosaic, a cached, versioned edit since June 2025; no measurement shows it adding resolution. Measured: Redlamp's X-Trans interpolation keeps half a clean edge's resolution (MTF50 0.185 against 0.347 cycles per pixel), the largest gap (CAM-07); on real noise at the highest ISO a learned raw denoiser beats Redlamp's best setting by 3.3 dB, RawNIND's joint model by 2.0 dB, classical denoising on the mosaic by 1.2 dB; the clamp at black tints deep shadows at very high ISO (DN-12). | DN-12 | Keep below-black values until after noise reduction: clamp after the detail stage rather than at normalisation, so noise in deep shadows averages to its true level (at ISO 51200-like noise the clamp lifts a dark patch's red by 39% and blue by 27%, a magenta cast and lifted blacks); hot photosites, highlights, the demosaic and the noise-stabilising transform must all accept negative values. A new process version | Do better | P2 | S | DN-02 | Accepted (2026-10-13): the owner chose it for 0.3.2 from the Lightroom parity review, in one new process version with CAM-29 and LNS-02's Panasonic and OM System corrections, which LNS-14 and LNS-16 joined on 14 October; (2026-10-14) shipped as it is, with noise reduction's own bias in noisy shadows proposed as DN-16; a photo built at the revision its edit needs when the editor knows it, since a second build for an older edit takes 0.64 s on a 24 MP raw | Done (commits "Values below black kept until after noise reduction: process 16 and raw revision 3" and "Process 16's golden renders of the bundled recipes, on both GPUs"): from process 16 (raw revision 3) the normalise kernel and every demosaic keep values below black, noise reduction's stabilising transform takes them by the read noise alone, and the develop kernel clamps once, after the detail stage. On DN-11's dark patch at ISO 51200-like noise the raw data average to within 3% of their level in every channel, where the clamp lifted red and blue by 15 to 20% and more; seen whole, the photo renders within 3% of a noise-free one, and at half size within 4% of neutral, where process 15 tints it magenta. Edits made before process 16 render as before (every version's references pass on both Macs); a photo is built at its edit's revision when the editor has read the edit in time (`EditingEngine.expect`), and otherwise an older edit renders from a variant. With Luminance at 60 at 1:1 the noisiest shadows still render 6 to 25% dark (DN-16) | [DN-11 §5.5](notes/DN-11-lightroom-raw-denoise.md#55-deep-shadows-and-the-clamp-at-black) |

First compare the same filter placed after the demosaic: the literature finds the order alone isn't what helps, but splitting denoising across it. Measured as a prototype: 0.4 to 1.2 dB over Redlamp's best setting on photos at high ISO, 1.2 to 5.8 dB on fine text, a third of the chroma noise. The filmstrip offers "Focus stack detected: N frames" with Merge, which saves a `.redlampstack` beside the frames; runs a document already covers aren't offered again. Finds the public R5 II stack and nothing among the 40 look-development raws or the fixtures (0.4 s for 40 files, warm). Vendor maker-note tags not started: LibRaw's maker-note callback passes a C++ stream, so it needs a small C++ shim | [AI §3.2](ai-findings.md#32-stack-detection); [Katami §5](notes/LIB-katami.md#5-proposed-tracker-changes) |
| FS-02 | Focus stacking v1: GPU alignment, focus measure, depth solve, streaming Auto/Smooth/Detail fusion, artifacts | Build | P3 | 9–11 ew | CAM-05, LNS-01, DN-01 | Accepted (2026-09-30): the full feature, developed on public raw stacks; frames are stacked as demosaiced camera RGB before any edit, so the result develops like a raw; a separate Stack workspace | In progress: alignment (ECC similarity on the CPU, chained from the narrowest view, Lanczos GPU warp, per-channel gains), the depth solve (sharpness measured in each frame's own geometry and then warped, at a quarter of a 2048-pixel analysis copy) and streaming Auto/Smooth/Detail fusion are done, with `redlamp stack`. On 25 frames of a public Canon R5 II series (every 40th of 999, CC BY 4.0): 8.1 s at 5118×3412 (decode 4.6 s with three frames decoding ahead of the GPU, align 3.0 s, depth 0.2 s, fusion 0.3 s; was 24 s), 2.3 GB peak (was 4.5); this smooth, low-texture surface leaves alignment poorly constrained (two ECC schedules with equal correlation differ by up to 300 px at the corners yet merge equally well), so alignment accuracy still needs a textured public raw stack. On the PoC's textured JPEG stacks: PCB, 7 frames (MIT): 3.6% breathing and 91% confident depth (PoC 3.7%, 91%) in 1.0 s (PoC 5.2 s); against `focus-stack`'s own result put through the same default develop, the gain over the sharpest frame matches the PoC (Smooth +8.4 dB, Auto +6.8, Detail +6.8; PoC +8.4, +6.3, +5.3). Mite in amber, 109 frames (CC BY 4.0): 65% confident depth (PoC 63%) in 14.6 s (PoC 97 s), 4.7 GB peak (was 10.5 before releasing each frame's GPU objects as it finishes); as sharp as the Python PoC at 1600 px (Tenengrad over the best frame: Auto 1.42 vs 1.35, Detail 1.96 vs 1.94, Smooth 1.39 vs 1.37). Depth artifacts: brightness is matched before measuring sharpness (focus breathing changes exposure; the brightest frame used to win wherever nothing was sharp: 8.8% of the defocused corner, now 0.02%), and where neither solve is confident the depth is filled from its surroundings (push-pull), so Smooth no longer blotches flat backgrounds; finally the depth map is smoothed along the image (guided by the sharpest-frame luma), so multi-peaked focus in transparent subjects (the mite in amber) no longer leaves depth-label seams in Smooth, at a small cost on the PCB (Smooth 24.09 dB and SSIM 0.866 against `focus-stack`, was 24.13 and 0.873, about 9% softer by Tenengrad; Auto and Detail unchanged). Clipped highlights count for nothing in the focus measure (a blown specular has the hardest edges in whichever frame clipped it; PCB Auto 22.60 dB and Smooth 24.17 dB against `focus-stack`, were 22.55 and 24.09). Lens corrections, the feature-matching fallback and halo handling beyond the Auto window not started | [AI §3.3](ai-findings.md#33-classical-pipeline-and-defaults) |

Each method's merge is cached, so switching back is instant; Done develops the stack in Develop. ## 10. Other AI workstreams

| ID | Item | Recommended | Phase | Size | Depends on | Decision | Status | Source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| RM-01 | Classical Heal and Clone (Poisson blending, exhaustive GPU source search, no PatchMatch) | Build | P3 | 5–7 ew | — | Accepted (2026-10-02): next in the plan the owner approved | Done: Heal and Clone spots render into a copy of the photo before everything else, saved with the edit and in Copy Settings (6b91624). Heal is Poisson blending solved without an iterative solver: the rim's log ratio of destination to source weighed by 1/d², which is the Poisson integral on a disc, after a median along the rim so a twig or wire crossing it doesn't bleed in. Each spot finds its own source (4916700): every place within ten radii, on the pyramid level where the spot is 8 texels across, matched on the rim and on the strength of its texture (a median absolute deviation, so thin things crossing the rim don't count), so it neither copies a flat area into texture nor takes another blemish. The Healing tool (`Q`, 6a6e488) adds a circle with a click and brushes a spot with a drag, moves spots and sources, resizes them, and sets Heal or Clone, Size, Feather and Opacity; `redlamp render --heal` and `--heal-brush` add them from the command line. A drag brushes a spot along a scratch, wire or twig, weighed over its outline the same way (0490993) | [AI §6](ai-findings.md#6-d-object-removal-healing-and-distraction-removal) |
| RM-02 | Dust detection and removal, Visualize Spots | Build | P3 | 3–4 ew | — | Accepted (2026-10-03): the owner asked for dust, specks and distraction removal | Done (b3fb8cc): Remove Dust (bb8a8d9) in the Healing tool finds sensor dust and heals each speck with an automatic source, in one step (and `redlamp render --remove-dust`). Specks are differences of Gaussians on log luminance at three scales, standing out by several times their neighbourhood's spread; dust is kept only where the photo is as smooth as its noise (the response noise alone would give), round, soft (its half depth well inside its tenth, unlike a pit or a stone), equally dark in every channel, with nothing dark crossing its surroundings and few specks near it. On the six sample raws only four dark spots on the Sony's smooth sandstone are found; dust added to the iPhone sample's sky is all found. Visualize Spots shows log luminance against a blur 8 times wider, in white on black, at a sensitivity. Across a shoot (66a9491): with several photos selected, Remove Dust looks at each (a little more keenly than for one photo), keeps the specks found at the same sensor place and about the same size in at least two of them and a fifth of the selection, and heals them in every photo from that sensor, the open one as a step of its own history and the rest as a batch Undo puts back. Evaluated (b3fb8cc) in place of a dusty sensor, which the owner doesn't have: realistic dust (shadows 3 to 12 pixels wide, 4% to 20% deep, a fifth of them fibres) multiplied into the raw data of 13 photos from 13 cameras, a shoot built from them binned to one sensor size, and six frames of a Nikon Z 6 on a tripod (`DustEvaluationTests`, samples by `mise run fixtures-shoots`). It found that a big speck raised its own bar (its neighbourhood's spread is now measured beyond its own reach), that fabric knots and marks in wood passed for dust (a speck with round look-alikes nearby, as dark, is the scene's), and that a tripod repeats the scene's specks as faithfully as dust (frames showing the same textured scene around a speck now count once). 12 of 16 visible round specks are found (11 before) with 0.38 false finds a frame (0.77); specks found in enough frames reach every frame; the Z 6's own dust is found in all six. Fibres mostly pass unfound (1 of 3), not being round | [AI §6](ai-findings.md#6-d-object-removal-healing-and-distraction-removal) |

| RM-04 | Inpainting data pipeline (PD12M, Megalith-10M) and mask generator | Build | P3 | 2–3 ew | — | Proposed | Not started | [AI §6](ai-findings.md#6-d-object-removal-healing-and-distraction-removal) |
| RM-05 | Train a LaMa-class inpainter; integrate crop-around-mask, caching and versioning | Build | P3→P4 | 9–12 ew + 1–3k GPU-hours | RM-04, DEC-06 | Proposed | Not started | [AI §6](ai-findings.md#6-d-object-removal-healing-and-distraction-removal) |
| RM-06 | iPhone inpainting student, Remove People, wire removal | Build | P4 | 10–14 ew | RM-05 | Proposed | Not started | [AI §6](ai-findings.md#6-d-object-removal-healing-and-distraction-removal) |
| RM-07 | Remove brush with content-aware fill: exemplar-based inpainting (Criminisi, Pérez and Toyama, 2004; patents expired) coarse to fine with an exhaustive GPU patch search (not PatchMatch), blended with Heal's Poisson seams; for litter, signs, wires and specks on natural texture | Build | P3 | L | RM-01 | Accepted (2026-10-03): the owner chose classical removal now, a learned inpainter later | Done (6d6260d): Remove spots, circles or brushed, in the Healing tool and `redlamp render --remove`. Each hole is filled on the pyramid level where it's at most 96 texels across, one 9 × 9 patch at a time in Criminisi's priority order (confidence times the isophote across the front), each patch the best of every candidate around the hole scored on the GPU; at full size each texel copies from where its patches came from, the four nearest blended, and Heal's rim blending matches the edge to the photo. Fills are cached per spot and the spots before it. On the samples, litter in grass disappears and a pot on a roof becomes roof with its seams continued. Large areas are better served by RM-10. Process 10 (b0f9656) makes Highlights and Shadows', Clarity's, Dehaze's and glow's maps again from the retouched photo, in the background for frames (about 0.1 s for 24 MP) and before rendering for stills and exports, so a removed object leaves no faint outline where they're used; edits from earlier processes keep the photo's | [AI §6](ai-findings.md#6-d-object-removal-healing-and-distraction-removal) |
| RM-08 | Click to pick what to remove: people (Vision's person instance masks) and objects (Segment Anything 2.1, Apache-2.0, as Objects masks use), filled by RM-07; and, under DEC-24, things named in words ("trash", "sign", "cable") found by an open-vocabulary detector with permissive weights (Florence-2, MIT, or OWLv2, Apache-2.0); SAM 3's own licence terms are still a separate question. Nothing ranks distractions automatically (US9665962B2 runs to 2035) | Build | P3 | M | RM-07, DEC-24 | Accepted (2026-10-03): the owner chose classical removal now | Done (a7042d2): in the Healing tool (384b23f), Click picks Person or Object; a click asks for the people masks (Vision) or an Objects mask at the point (Segment Anything 2.1) and adds a Remove spot shaped by that mask, grown by its radius (0.5% of the height to begin with) and feathered, filled as RM-07 fills. The mask is saved with the edit like an AI mask's. Things named in words: OWLv2 B/16 (Apache-2.0) is converted to Core ML (66124d1), computing in float32 (in float16 a few dozen patches a photo drift through its massive activations, and 8-bit weights move some boxes by over half the square), at 150 ms a photo on the GPU and matching PyTorch on 40 renders; the text features of 19 prompts for 13 things (the owner's list) are precomputed, so no text encoder ships. It is published as the prerelease models-owlv2-base-v1 (2ef16c9; never the app's Latest) and offered in Settings › Models under DEC-24, whose lineage verdict the licence gate now has. In the Healing tool, Find (a7042d2) outlines what OWLv2 finds (one thing or all 13), and a click on one asks Segment Anything 2.1 for its mask from its box (SAM's box prompt) and removes it with a Remove spot, as a picked object; Remove All removes everything outlined as one step, and `redlamp render --remove-found car` does it from the command line. On the D7500 sample the red car is found where it's parked (0.41) and its box gives SAM the whole car. Filled by RM-07, something as large as a car leaves a smeared patch: areas that size are RM-10's | [AI §6](ai-findings.md#6-d-object-removal-healing-and-distraction-removal) |
| RM-09 | Survey open-source inpainting and open-vocabulary detection models whose code, weights and training data allow shipping on device | Adopt | P3 | S | — | Accepted (2026-10-03): the owner asked to check open models before training any | Done (957dfb5): [open-model findings](open-model-findings.md), every licence read from its primary source. No small inpainter trained on clean data exists; MI-GAN's weights are MIT since 14 September 2026, the one to retrain for RM-05 (5.9M parameters). FLUX.2 [klein] 4B is the one shippable generative inpainter (Apache-2.0 weights and text encoder, undisclosed data, about 8B parameters with its encoder: Mac only), see DEC-23. Segment Anything 2.1 and EdgeTAM are clean apart from ImageNet-pretrained encoders; SAM 3's licence allows commercial use but must travel with the weights. Every open-vocabulary detector with permissive weights (Florence-2, OWLv2, Grounding DINO, Falcon Perception) has research-only data in its lineage, see DEC-24 | [open-model findings](open-model-findings.md) |
| RM-10 | Opt-in generative fill on Macs: FLUX.2 [klein] 4B (Apache-2.0) through MLX, removal only, about a 2.4 GB download (the transformer and VAE; removal prompts precomputed, so no text encoder ships), for regions too large for RM-07, labelled as generated (RM-03) | Adopt | P3 | M | DEC-23, RM-07 | Accepted (2026-10-03): the owner said yes to DEC-23; (2026-10-05) removal only, without the text encoder | Done (2026-10-06, 5ba126b): the model is published as the prerelease models-flux2-klein-4b-fill-v1, once the owner approved the upload (DEC-26); its eight files download from there and match the manifest, so the Healing tool offers Generative on Macs with 16 GB or more, and Report a Bug lists Generative Remove. Step 1 (4512f76): mlx-swift (DEC-25) in a Mac-only RedlampGenerative module, with Qwen3-4B's first 27 layers making FLUX.2 [klein]'s prompt embeddings as transformers does (within 0.1% in float32). Step 2 (e0d4e6d): the transformer, the VAE, the schedule and Flux2KleinInpaintPipeline's loop in MLX, matching diffusers in float32 from the same noise (whole fill above 40 dB on crops of two CC0 photos). On an M1 Ultra at 512 px and 4 steps, 4-bit weights (2.22 GB) fill in 8 to 10 s, as bfloat16 (7.75 GB) and 8-bit (4.15 GB) do, and stay 41 to 46 dB from float32 inside the mask, indistinguishable by eye; 4-bit ships, about 4.6 GB in use while filling. The pipeline passes the photo, the thing to remove included, as a reference, so Generative Remove hides it first. Step 3 (a1b545e): the download, 2.41 GB (4-bit transformer in shards under 2 GB, the VAE, three removal prompts encoded by diffusers' encode_prompt), filling exactly as the model quantised at load does; the model store resumes interrupted downloads and reports progress within a file; a manifest's minimum memory (16 GB until measured) keeps it from smaller Macs; the licence gate takes undisclosed data under DEC-23 only, in CI. Step 4 (5172060): Generative Remove in the engine: a Remove spot's fill kept with the edit as the photo's camera RGB in a 16-bit PNG, rendered with the photo's noise and Heal's rim blending, the same on any Mac, and ignored by builds that don't know it; the model reaches the engine through a protocol, so the engine and iOS never link MLX. What the model is shown decides the result: on the D7500's car the photo itself brings the car back and no reference invents another, while the spot filled from the photo, prompted to remove, gives a clean driveway on three seeds, the default ([design](../plans/2026-10-05-generative-fill-design.md)). On water some of content-aware fill's streaks survive. Step 5 (8172c08): the Healing tool: Remove's Fill is Content-Aware or Generative, shown only where the model is offered, and the first use offers the download with its size, licence and that fills are labelled as generated; a new Remove spot, clicked, brushed, picked or found, gets three fills (Remove All one each) with progress and Cancel, arrows between them, More, and Content-Aware to go back; a filled spot is labelled Generated on the canvas and in the panel, and its steps in History; moving or resizing it drops the fill, which no longer fits, and fills it again; closing the tool lets the model's memory go. Step 6 (13f033d): on the M1 Ultra a fill takes 11 s at 512 pixels, 24 s at 768 and 46 s at 1024, with the model loaded in 3 s; the car, in a Release build, 40 s. A 1024-pixel fill peaked at 13.1 GB, nearly all of it the VAE's convolutions, which MLX unfolds into a copy per kernel tap: convolving large inputs in bands and fusing the norms with SiLU brings it to 6.6 GB with the same fills as diffusers, and capping MLX's cache keeps what the app holds near that, for 7% more time. The minimum stays 16 GB, now measured ([design](../plans/2026-10-05-generative-fill-design.md#what-a-fill-costs)). Fill times on other Macs are still to measure | [open-model findings](open-model-findings.md) |

On the duck sample the teal's bill no longer turns up in the mallard's fill; most of what's left there comes from feather tips and spray just outside the masks, and from RM-12's blocks | Found on the look-dev duck sample (Canon EOS 7D Mark II) while fixing RM-07's trace (b0f9656) |
| RM-12 | Content-aware fill refined coarse to fine: the moves found on the working level are searched again, exhaustively in a small window around themselves, on each finer level (hierarchical block matching, not PatchMatch), so the fill keeps the photo's texture at full size instead of blocks of the working level and the doubled copies of blending four of them; on water the blocks show as vertical streaks | Build | P3 | M | RM-07 | Accepted (2026-10-04): the owner chose fill quality before generative fill | Not started. Tried and not shipped (2026-10-04): searching the working level's moves again on finer levels (each texel's own and its four neighbours' moves, a texel either way, and neighbours up to 16 texels away) keeps the working level's blocks, since they're where each coarse patch copies from, and softened the duck sample's fill. A car a third of the frame across copies sky, doors and walls into the drive at any mask growth (0.5 to 2.5% of the height): holes that size need RM-10. Tried and not shipped (2026-10-05): a finer working level, made affordable by keeping the front's priorities in a heap updated only near each patch (81ce156, which fills exactly as before and ships): with holes up to 160 or 256 texels across instead of 96 the duck sample's water still shows the mallard's vertical ghost and copies of the other duck, and at 1:1 its texture is the same, for 1.5 to 2.6 times the time, so no process version. What's left is the source: water near the hole is the ducks' reflections, which RM-13's reflection regions would take out of the candidates; and a wider search on finer levels, checked against DEC-05 | Found on the look-dev duck sample (Canon EOS 7D Mark II) while fixing RM-07's trace (b0f9656) |
| RM-13 | A picked person or object's cast shadow and reflection removed with it: a classical first pass (the region beside the mask darker than its surroundings in the same colour, and on water the mirror image below it), then open instance-shadow models checked under DEC-24 | Build | P3 | M | RM-08 | Accepted (2026-10-05): the owner chose it for the masking plan | Done (2026-10-05, 2b186e7): a person or object picked with a click, or removed from Find or `--remove-found`, takes its cast shadow and its reflection with it, under the Healing tool's Shadows and reflections checkbox (on by default; `--keep-shadows` in the CLI). The shadow is ground touching the thing from its lowest quarter down, darker than 0.6 of the ground around it in the ground's colour or a cooler one (lit by the sky alone), and bordering lit ground along most of its edge. It is bounded to a quarter of the thing's width beyond either end and a third of its size below it, since shade from something else that it touches is as dark and as blue: the D7500 car sample's front shadow goes and the house's shade beside the car stays. The reflection is the thing mirrored below its lowest point, kept as deep as the photo there takes on the thing's colour against what lies beside it, rippled or not: three of the look-dev duck sample's four ducks lose theirs, and the fourth's pale reflection stays. Both are found on a grid at most 1024 pixels across. Telling a thing's own shadow from shade it touches: RM-14 | Found on the look-dev duck sample (Canon EOS 7D Mark II) while fixing RM-07's trace (b0f9656) |

The separator is the denoiser at 3 sigmas on luma and chroma (luma alone let flat noise grow 17–29%); 4 Richardson–Lucy iterations; Detail blends unsharp mask and deconvolution. Calibrated in `shp01_calibrate.py`: at Radius 2 / Amount 100 / Detail 100, +1.7 dB on mild softness and +1.2 dB on noisy defocus, where the old unsharp mask lost 0.9 dB; flat noise changes under 2%. What remains: (1) first, before any pack is uploaded, the packs' shared namespace: the system merges every pack into one folder, five manifests (vitmatte-base, owlv2-base, sam3, depth-anything-3-mono-large and flux2-klein-4b-fill) have a top-level `LICENSE.txt`, FLUX's first file is that `LICENSE.txt`, and `location(of:)` resolves a pack's root from its first file without naming the pack, so one pack's root can resolve into another's, and a model's next version would collide with its last: each pack laid out under `<id>/<version>/`, with files looked up within their own pack; (2) a downloader extension target (Apple-hosted, managed) conforming to StoreKit's `StoreDownloaderExtension`, with its own App ID and profile; (3) an app group shared by the app and the extension, which needs the sandbox (ARC-17), and the Info.plist keys `BAHasManagedAssetPacks`, `BAUsesAppleHosting` and `BAAppGroupID`, with no other Background Assets keys (the group ID's format on macOS is unverified); (4) a `ba-package` manifest per model version (`onDemand`, macOS), archives built by a script from the published files, and uploads with altool or Transporter, submitted for review with the build; (5) download progress from `statusUpdates(forAssetPackWithID:)`, since `ensureLocalAvailability` reports none and the masks' and Settings' progress bars would stay at 0; (6) older versions' packs removed with `remove(assetPackWithID:)`, since `removeOutdated` deletes only directly downloaded folders, and MSK-33's move from ViTMatte version 1 to version 2 relies on it; (7) packs only for cleared models, with ViTMatte at version 2 only; (8) tests against Apple's local mock server; and no fallback to the publisher in the App Store build (DEC-97), with the download prompts that give each size kept. Seven packs, about 4.3 GB in all, against limits of 200 packs and 200 GB | Build | P4 | 2–3 ew | INF-01, ARC-17, ARC-22, DEC-93, DEC-97 | Accepted (2026-10-15): the owner asked for an App Store build that complies with every guideline, with the fixes built | In progress: `ModelStore` uses a pack per model version in builds that declare managed asset packs, and otherwise downloads from the publisher with SHA-256 checks; a size-disclosed prompt and Settings › Models. ## 13. Library and catalog

The library and catalog track (5 October 2026): folders on disk organise photos, each photo's metadata stays in its sidecar, and an index on the Mac keeps a library of a million photos instant on local, spinning and network disks (DEC-42 to DEC-49). LIB-01 to LIB-04 come first: the research study, the measured design and the stress harness, whose budgets gate every merge. The core ships in 1.0; LIB-31 to LIB-35 and LIB-43 come after it. The research (LIB-01) refined the rows, split exact duplicates (LIB-39) from LIB-31 and added LIB-36 to LIB-38; the owner accepted its changes on 5 October 2026. A study of Katami ([LIB-katami](notes/LIB-katami.md), 6 October 2026) added Library Health (LIB-40), moments and grouping (LIB-41), soft frames in bursts without a model (LIB-42, split from OTH-02, its phase waiting on a measurement against the owner's own culls), sensor dust followed across shoots (LIB-43) and Open With from Finder (UX-37), and reworded LIB-06, LIB-18, LIB-23, LIB-26, LIB-34, LIB-38, OTH-02 and FS-01; the owner asked for it to be folded into the catalog work the same day. A study of Cling, a fuzzy file finder for the Mac ([LIB-cling](notes/LIB-cling.md), 7 October 2026), added the column store mapped from a snapshot (LIB-44) and text that ignores accents and width everywhere (DEC-52), and reworded LIB-06, LIB-18 and LIB-19; the owner accepted its changes the same day.

| ID | Item | Recommended | Phase | Size | Depends on | Decision | Status | Source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| LIB-01 | Library research study: what photographers rely on in Lightroom Classic's Library, Capture One, Photo Mechanic, Bridge, darktable, digiKam, Apple Photos and the culling and DAM tools, and the complaints that recur most, ranked with sources, into `docs/research/library-findings.md` and the Library module inventory in the Lightroom feature inventory | Build | P4 | M | DEC-46 | Accepted (2026-10-05): the owner asked for competitors and common complaints to be researched | Done (2026-10-10, landed on main in 350bc6e8): four notes, the findings and the Library inventory in the Lightroom feature inventory (43f8983), and the tracker changes they proposed, which the owner accepted: LIB-36 to LIB-39, SKIP-19 to SKIP-21 and the wording of 17 rows. | The owner (2026-10-05) |
| LIB-02 | Library design, measured with the stress harness: the index and query engine, the thumbnail and preview store, I/O per volume, change detection, photo lists, the modules and how they switch, sidecar location and the sidecar's new fields, the keyboard and mouse map, and the file-operations journal | Build | P4 | M | LIB-01, LIB-03 | Accepted (2026-10-05): the owner's request | In progress: the architecture (a8e5153) and the first measured results (e0171ee), which grow as each row is measured | The owner (2026-10-05) |
| LIB-03 | Library stress harness: synthetic libraries of 100,000 to 2,000,000 photos (APFS clones of the CC0 raws with their capture dates rewritten, small files with varied EXIF, GPS and IPTC, sidecars and other apps' `.xmp`, folder shapes from date trees to 20,000 photos in one folder) with a manifest of what each query must return, and a file layer that simulates spinning disks and networks (latency, bandwidth, jitter, disconnects), with cold-cache runs on a disk image | Build | P4 | L | DEC-47 | Accepted (2026-10-05): the owner asked for a stress harness that demonstrates the performance and catches regressions | In progress: seeded fixtures with a manifest of expected counts, simulated SSD, spinning disk, NAS, Wi-Fi and VPN volumes, `redlamp library fixture` (9ac6ba7 to 345a8d6); the 1M fixture, measured in the library design's M1 run, and the 2M, measured on fd1d4d6a (12 October, the design's Results): all 43 counts right, search p95 7.7 ms, facets p95 7.9 ms and a launch from the column store's snapshot in 36 ms, while three of the budgets set for a million miss at two million: listing every photo (49 s), a first launch that builds the store (1.29 s, 486 MB) and the first 1,000 photos indexed (11.0 s). Not done: cold-cache runs (they need the owner's terminal), a real network share | The owner (2026-10-05) |
| LIB-04 | Library benchmarks and regression gates: `redlamp library bench` (a cold index build, a warm launch, reconciling after changes, search as you type, facets and sorts, bulk writes, an interrupted rename, a volume vanishing) and `--library-perf` in the app (grid scrolling, held arrow keys in every view, switching modules, culling 10,000 photos, files arriving, memory through each phase), each with a PASS/FAIL budget, differential checks of query results, and metrics on redlamp.app/performance | Build | P4 | L | LIB-03 | Accepted (2026-10-05): the owner's request | In progress: `redlamp library bench` with PASS/FAIL budgets (listing, the fixture check, index-build, warm-launch, reconcile, vanishing-volume, search, facets, store, lists, naming, duplicates) and the app's `--library-perf` (cd612b6, da89d73: warm launch, a folder in the filmstrip, held arrow keys, memory); `bench --index` keeps a built index between runs, and `library-launch` now measures the library visible and searchable, with reconciling apart. Not done: `--library-perf` on 20,000 photos on a quiet disk, the metrics on redlamp.app/performance | The owner (2026-10-05) |
| LIB-05 | Library index: macOS's own SQLite (no package) in RedlampDocument, with a schema and migrations, WAL with one writer and read connections, never on the main thread and always on the Mac's own disk; snapshots and integrity checks while it runs; rebuildable from the photos and their sidecars at any time | Build | P4 | L | DEC-42, LIB-02 | Accepted (2026-10-05): in the core the owner chose for 1.0 (DEC-46) | Done (2026-10-10, landed on main in 350bc6e8): built (ce09390 to 73e137c; schema version 2 with LIB-06): at a million photos a path lookup in 8 µs and a text search in 0.2 ms. A root being removed is marked in the settings table (`library.removing.<id>`), so reads leave its photos out before its rows are swept, without a schema change (3d6bec9). Photo, folder and root IDs are never given twice, from counters in the settings table, so a batch Undo, which writes sidecars by photo ID, can't reach a photo added after a removal (67ec7eb). Collection, keyword, camera and lens IDs never repeat either, and the last IDs are kept beside the index in `Index.ids`, synced in the transaction that gives them, so an index restored from a snapshot or rebuilt from nothing gives none again (5d95214, 8c8d105, 6842fa7); Put Back finds collections by path and reads again a photo whose camera or lens the index doesn't have, and a sidecar move resumes by its root's path. The writer's commits don't checkpoint: a connection of its own does, keeping the full syncs, and a write waits only once the log reaches 32,768 pages (53fe614); schema version 8 adds an index on the photos with sidecars by folder (49a10ea); syncing `Index.ids` costs about 10% of an index build at a million (1.77 ms per 1,000-photo transaction); since, `Index.ids` is reserved a block ahead (65,536 photos, 4,096 folders, 256 for the rest), synced only when an ID passes the mark and settled 2 s after the last write, so the build at a million is 12% faster and a crash skips at most a block per table (89fa762); the text index is merged in steps of about 8 ms after writes of text, FTS5's automerge off for every writer and a write returning once no level holds 8 segments (1c16fe6, d89c307): with keyword batches running another write waits p99 54 ms from 1,284, search staying at p95 0.1 to 0.6 ms; and checkpoints run every 256 pages, halving the long waits a checkpoint's sync causes (2a0f1af). The library stops its weekly check when it closes, which had run 60 s later on the closed index and failed with a disk I/O error (d9a4bd0). Schema version 11: `missing_since` and an index of the missing photos by folder (3516ad5). `open()` is retried when a signal interrupts it, as `pread` was (780eacc). | The owner (2026-10-05) |

For the filter bar (064ebd0): `missing:` and `offline:`, sorts by modification date and file size, counts per metadata column, filtered lists, a rule form and completions; the column store 9 bytes a photo larger. Custom labels (and a label set's names for their colours), `collection:`, IPTC Core's creator, copyright and location as filters, `has:` and free text, matched against small tables of names so indexing isn't slower; traits `is:long-exposure`, `is:panorama`, `is:high-resolution`, `is:low-light` and `is:no-location`, with `megapixels:` and `aspect:`, offered in completion with counts (4236823 to 196af34). At a million photos search stays at p95 2.5 ms and facets at 6.0 to 10.6 ms; the column store is 87.5 bytes a photo and loads in 459 to 634 ms (from 323 to 337). Text beyond ASCII folded once and matched by bytes: a keystroke over 5,604 accented folders in p95 0.38 to 0.78 ms, from 45 ms (c6249d5). `orientation:` and a time of day in `date:` (e017d7c, d2dad40). Text ignores accents and width everywhere (DEC-52): the small tables, `kw:`, `collection:` and the text index, built again as schema version 7 in 5.7 to 9.6 s at a million (7ab372a, 292cbc5); free text under three characters still matched against the small tables (1ebf9e8), its lookups in one pass (6446dc5); search p95 at a million 3.7 to 3.9 ms. A trait for the moments without a pick, `is:unpicked-moment`, evaluated in one pass over the source's photos in capture order and kept for each version of the store, source and setting (71cad5b). `is:damaged` is found without Library Health's full check and kept across store changes, at p95 2.6 to 3.0 ms after a change (b527caf). The name order is Folders' own, one `FileOrder` key for both, in the column store, the SQL collation, facets, keyword lists, collections and groups (d1bc4bf); names with accents or other non-ASCII letters sort ignoring case, accents and width; the column snapshot is format 2, rebuilt once at the next launch; Lightroom keyword files are written in that order. The name order is the Finder's (DEC-58), one `FileOrder` key for the library and Folders (7e4cd96): punctuation before digits (DSC_5513 before DSC05507), numbers by value, then accents, case and leading zeros, width and kana; every pair of 1,000 names agrees with `localizedStandardCompare`, the Finder's own inconsistencies listed in `FileOrderTests`; the column snapshot is format 3; Lightroom keyword files come out in Lightroom's own order. The traits Wide Open (within a sixth of a stop of the lens's widest aperture at its focal length), Telephoto (70 mm or longer in 35 mm terms) and Ultra Wide (shorter than 24 mm), with the fields `widest:` and `focal35:` in facets, the filter bar's columns, smart collections and `redlamp library search`, from the lens's widest aperture and its 35 mm-equivalent focal length kept in the index since schema 12: the aperture from the lens's name, then EXIF's lens specification, then MaxApertureValue; the focal length from EXIF's own tag, else a crop factor by make and model (Canon EOS, OM System and Olympus, Leica, Phase One), else the focal-plane resolution. The migration fills both from what the index keeps (7.4 to 8.5 s on lib-1m) and reads again only the photos it can't; at a million photos the traits' first page and count in p95 5.7 ms and their facets in p95 5.1 ms (0f2c56de to af048aee; scenario `library.lens-traits`). | The owner (2026-10-05); [LIB §5](library-findings.md#5-recommendations); [Katami §5](notes/LIB-katami.md#5-proposed-tracker-changes); [LIB-cling](notes/LIB-cling.md) |

At a million (dda3add to 2d466e5): a crash about a thousand batches in fixed (each commit nested a closure one level deeper); all 1,000,000 photos indexed in 2,153 s, 464 a second at a load average of 80, with no failures and a peak of 1.33 GB; the first 1,000 searchable in 3.5 s, from 30.7 s, once listings stopped going ahead of every photo read; the folders on screen really first (88f4884: other folders' photos had been read before the folder on screen was listed). Other apps' metadata is read with LibraryXMP's own code, field by field (0c46000). The two races Thread Sanitizer flagged weren't races: the Swift runtime doesn't tell it about the ordering it gives a read that finished before its caller waited; results now also pass through a lock, and its runs are clean (eed4a7a). A change only the `.redlamp` has reads no photo again: a keyword on 10,000 photos is indexed in 15 to 23 s with none read again, where 3,369 were in 49 to 53 s (29ef597); with schema version 4's fields, the 20,000-photo index build took 20.0 and 20.9 s, against 32.5 and 23.4 before. Keyword and metadata batches keep the index's date for sidecars they leave as they are, and the XMP sync records the dates of the sidecars it writes, so change tracking reads none of them again (e763c1f to c2748ac). The folders asked for finish before other folders start (2569f21), and another app's shifted capture time is read from its `.xmp` (2d9d4b5). An indexing batch no longer merges the text index in its commit (1c16fe6): at a million a batch is held p95 27 ms from 32, and the build from nothing to a million takes 23.0 s from 25.7. A batch's change to a photo stands over what change tracking read before it (8066a55, 82432cd): metadata and keyword batches register their photos from their first index write until their sidecars' dates are recorded, and the indexer writes a read only if the photo isn't registered and its row is as its listing found it, otherwise reading it again in the same run once the batch is done, so another app's `.xmp` and another Mac's `.redlamp` still arrive; `IndexerStaleReadTests` failed before, losing a keyword, a collection, and a rating and label for good. Text merges take each transaction's result in the order they ran, so a merge step's late result can't end a later write's wait early (020af78); a new test fails without it. On a disk that spins, or an external one that doesn't say, one reader that never widens, the index recording the disk as spinning or unknown, and a Lightroom import's root folders read first on the lane Low Power Mode doesn't stop (LIB-53: 3553a897c, 1637d50a1, 86ab44378). Not done: the 2 s first-results budget, to check on a quiet Mac; a signal 11 seen once in a warm launch at 400,000 photos, not reproduced since. | The owner (2026-10-05) |
