Three runs on the 2007 folder left no sidecar changed after Undo, culling's main thread at p99 1.4 to 2.0 ms. The grid's budgets (b803bc7c): a list takes `LibraryLive`'s next update once the main thread has applied the last, so a step at 20,000 photos arrives as 335 to 1,762 changes rather than one each; on the 2007 folder a step is on screen within 5.8 ms, its p99 1.6 ms, and at 20,000 its main thread's p99 is 5.8 ms; the stall of 7 to 12 s is gone (LIB-14). The budgets' second round (52c5fa42, 14a413e8): a batch's badges in one pass, its rows as ranges, LibraryLive's rows found by ID, the selection counted without a walk, the steps Undo drops freed in the background; at 20,000 a step on screen in 4.8 to 5.8 ms after an Undo (from 21), the main thread's p99 1.1 to 1.4 ms. Undo leaves sidecars as they were (a5a464c, 3846fae): the app's XMP sync copied other apps' captions, keywords, creators and labels into a photo's new `.redlamp` on its first sync, so Undo left one on photos that had none, hundreds a run; two runs on the 2007 folder and two at 20,000 photos left no sidecar changed after Undo. Auto Advance follows the grid's order, its groups and stacks, and the selection is read once for the menus' checks (068a918, 4ad1072). ⌘Z and ⇧⌘Z take back and make again the library's changes in the one order they were made, culling's, the panels', renames' and moves' and Put Back's alike, from one clock per editor, Develop's own Undo unchanged (6c67ad1); every culling change reaches the photos' sidecars whatever source is shown, and in Library a culling key with no photo listed to reach isn't offered (e7f317f); `selectedPhotos` reads the selection once (e523c14). Library's Undo keeps 20 steps across every kind together, the oldest dropped first (f236974). Culling a whole large selection takes its IDs, reading only its URLs and the five values its badges show off the main thread, with the same result (44fc874). Custom labels' colours, named and recoloured in a Custom Labels sheet (the Photo menu, a cell's menu and the palette), kept in each sidecar (`customLabelColor`) and the index (schema 13), and shown wherever labels show; a change that can't be written to some photos shown in a strip over them, naming how many and why, with Select, Try Again and Close; B on the target collection as DEC-62 chose; a custom label from the Photo menu reaching only Compare's and Survey's active photo (0a59e8b7 to 38791bd8; scenarios `library.custom-label-colors`, `library.culling-problem`, `library.target-collection-b`). A folder opened by another form of its path (`//`, `.`, `..`, `/private`) now names its photos as the index does, so culling reaches them, as CI's temporary folder showed (fdb3a94e; scenario `library.folder-by-another-path`). Develop's open photo keeps culling's changes shown when a save merges another write, and a change made from Library to it is written to its sidecar too (c64e92b0). Auto Advance kept for each view, on in the loupe and off in the grid, where a key acts on a selection, until it's chosen there, Compare, Survey and Develop starting with it off; the Photo menu's item naming the view it sets, and a button in the loupe's caption (DEC-68; e659915f, 424b33e4; scenario `library.loupe-auto-advance`). Not done: the first step at 20,000 under a high load (13.7 ms); custom labels in the filter bar's Attribute row; their colours in `.xmp`. | The owner (2026-10-05) |

Recently Trashed in the app (b9e5972f): a source under the folders with its count, its photos in the grid and filmstrip from the store, with Put Back for a photo, the selection or a batch from the context menu, the Photo menu, the palette and ⌘⌫, through the journal; its photos stay in Library, never opened in Develop, with editing and culling off. Rename Photos and Move to Folder in the app (e09b8123 to 25877c75): a live preview naming every photo off the main thread, empty tokens flagged and collisions numbered in capture order; one journaled batch with progress, photos keeping their IDs so the selection follows; Undo and Redo on ⌘Z and ⇧⌘Z in turn with culling's; Move to Folder from an Open panel that refuses folders outside the library; Put Back undone and redone on ⌘Z and ⇧⌘Z through the journal (c533906b). Photos dragged onto a folder move there as one batch with Undo, its progress in the grid's toolbar, and a drop on their own folder or one outside the library is refused (07740ed, f795d31); a 1,000-photo drop at main-thread p99 4.9 to 5.7 ms. ⌘Z is there in the turn a drop's move ends (a6c18de), and renames, moves and Put Backs take their place on Undo when they're asked for. Library Health's batches go to the Trash through the file journal, with Undo and Put Back, and Recently Trashed follows big batches without `lstat` on the main thread (7ee7da9). ⌘Z and ⇧⌘Z while a drop's move runs wait for it, then take it back and make it again (9783abd). A batch holds the folders its steps change, once listings of them already under way are done, until its index is written, and the indexer lists or probes a held folder only after that; the index's writes delete a vanished row only from the folder it vanished from and drop a late write for a row a batch has moved since (ce1cf61, 8ae4e81). Moved photos keep their IDs, edits, keywords and collections through a move, its Undo and its Redo, whatever change tracking reads meanwhile: `FileBatchListingTests` failed before, A and C getting new IDs and Redo moving neither. Copying and Stop (860f00f to 9dbcdc1): ⌥ on a drop and Copy to Folder… copy photos as a journaled batch finished or rolled back at launch, each copy with a new ID, its original's sidecars and none of its collections, a taken name numbered as the Finder does, and Undo moving the copies to the Trash; renames, moves and copies, and their Undo and Redo, stop after the photo in hand, leaving one Undo step for what was done. Copies are clones on their own volume and on another read once as they're copied, then read back: 1,000 raws (32.5 GB) within the SSD in 5.3 s from 570.8, to the internal disk in 123.1 s from 356.9 (db30125); moves across volumes read the original once; Folders takes a batch's listings together each turn with counts in place, and a rename's or move's URLs are found off the main thread (113a12c, 5837cde), renaming, moving and the rename's Undo at main-thread p99 1.75 to 1.92 ms; `redlamp library copy` (73d8c40); a stopped rename's Undo takes out the original names only of the photos it put back (787dad2). Copy to Folder… is in the Photo menu (5288d7d). Move and Copy to Folder show their progress and Stop in the grid's toolbar, as a drop, an Undo and a Redo do (55afc61): a move of 1,000 photos in 2.5 s with no main-thread turn over 34 ms, where its sheet cost two turns of about 400 ms; Rename Photos writes its presets file off the main thread (deb86d6); a folder never shows a photo twice or where it was while a batch moves it (f712231). Photos dragged from the filmstrip onto a folder or a collection, as from the grid (ef836313). Not done: speed against the 1 s budget, which this Mac's disk can't meet; every sheet (Rename Photos, Move Edits) holding the main thread about 0.3 s as it opens and as it closes, in AppKit's run loop, whether they stay sheets the owner's call; the move's Undo of 1,000 photos at p99 5.8 to 8.6 ms, 3 or 4 turns of 42 to 58 ms in the grid, the filmstrip and SwiftUI. | The owner (2026-10-05); [LIB §5](library-findings.md#5-recommendations); [Katami §5](notes/LIB-katami.md#5-proposed-tracker-changes) |
| LIB-27 | Import from cards and cameras: a card browsed at once from its embedded previews, and while it copies; several cards at once; folder and name templates; two destinations, the backup a real copy and never an APFS clone; copies verified before a card can be erased; photos already imported recognised from file data and a partial hash before previews are read, and skipped; a raw-only switch; ratings and flags given on the card written at the destination; metadata presets and keywords applied; a start when a card is inserted; the card ejected; one ingest path with tethered capture (TET-01) | Adopt | P4 | L | LIB-25, LIB-07, DEC-45 | Accepted (2026-10-05): in the core the owner chose for 1.0 (DEC-46) | Done (2026-10-11, fb082fc to d64cfd5, aeb7109c to e39ec752): cards told from other volumes and browsed from their embedded previews before anything is copied; photos already imported skipped by content key; folder and name templates; a backup that's a real copy; every copy read back and matched by size and SHA-256 before a card is safe to erase; the choices made while browsing written at the destination; journaled and resumable; one ingest step for tethered capture to share; `redlamp library import`. The import window (705dbc7 to 8d7a913, 130e9d8): File › Import Photos… (⇧⌘I), opening by itself when a card is inserted; cards and folders browsed from their previews, those already imported left out, and culled with Library's keys before copying; templates with a live example, a backup, raw-only and keywords; one journaled run off the main thread with Cancel and Resume; each card safe to erase once verified, with Eject; Library showing the photos imported, selected. First previews on screen in 186 and 197 ms, the window's grid at main-thread p99 3.4 to 4.3 ms while copying 2,000 raws. Metadata presets in the window, their ticked fields written at the destination by mode (975ab88e). A shown Previous Import follows an import that finishes, its photos selected (26497ae). An import cancelled or cut short is rolled back whole as well as resumed: its copies at the destination and the backup to the Trash while each is still the copy it made, its photos out of the library and the folders it made removed once empty, journaled so that a rollback cut short finishes, the card's files never touched (`redlamp library import --list` and `--roll-back`); Library shows a resumed import's photos selected; a card of 2,000 JPEGs of 10 MB shows its first preview in 83 to 85 ms (from 266 to 280) and its first 40 in 211 to 234 ms (from 4.65 s), each photo from its file's head first (aeb7109c to e39ec752; scenarios `import.roll-back`, `import.roll-back-interrupted`, `import.resume`, `import.jpeg-previews`). Rolling back an import that finished is in the library and on the command line; offering it in the app is the owner's call. | The owner (2026-10-05); [LIB §5](library-findings.md#5-recommendations) |
| LIB-28 | Stacks: raw and JPEG pairs as one photo, bursts stacked by sub-second capture time and exposure length (and by similarity with LIB-31), manual stacks across folders, saved in each photo's sidecar and shown in every view, and focus-stack suggestions in the grid | Adopt | P4 | M | LIB-10 | Accepted (2026-10-10): built; bursts start open (DEC-61, DEC-64) | Done (2026-10-11, 495ce8c to 7fe9569, e2206239, ef836313): pairs, bursts and focus-stack suggestions found from the index alone, manual stacks across folders, every list able to show its stacks closed and open them with diffs, and `redlamp library stacks`; at a million photos every stack found in 62 to 87 ms. Manual stacks are in each photo's sidecar (`stack`, its `top` written only when true) and the index's stacks are built from them, so a rebuilt index keeps them, with the index's earlier stack settings migrated; stack, unstack and choosing the top are journaled batches with Undo, `redlamp library stacks stack`, `unstack` and `top` (c390ead to d891b4a). In the app (78b59cf0 to 3bc6fe3e): the grid and the filmstrip show a closed stack as one cell with its count, inside Group By's groups too, and raw and JPEG pairs as one photo; Group into Stack (⌘G), Unstack (⇧⌘G), Move to Top of Stack (⇧S), Open or Close Stack (S) and Open All or Close All Stacks, in the menus, the grid's and the filmstrip's menus and the palette, each with Undo; a closed stack's selection, culling and keywording reach all its photos, and culling's advance steps past it; burst and focus-stack suggestions shown. A photo's copies are in no burst (c2fbc13): the same folder, camera and moment, and a name that comes from the other's, so an export beside its original stays unstacked, a default the owner can reverse. Found bursts start open, every frame shown and the burst's count on its first cell, each source keeping how its stacks start; pairs and stacks made by hand start closed (DEC-64, e2206239; scenario `library.bursts-open`). Release, on the 2007 folder and at 20,000: one stack opened or closed at a median of 1.4 to 1.8 ms, p95 1.5 to 2.3 ms against 2; the frames while toggling at p99 6.0 to 7.3 ms against 8.3; every stack found at a million in 65 to 67 ms. A crash they brought is fixed (ac9e0d5): on a change of stacks the filmstrip reloaded without counting its photos, so one arriving in the same turn was counted twice by a batch update, which raised and quit the app when a filter emptied the list; a reload now counts them at once. Stacks' readers leave a removed root's photos out until it's swept (1d8819b). A list without stacks before or after a change isn't restacked, so a filter no longer reloads the filmstrip (3242d5e). Which stacks are open is kept with each source's view, across launches (3c9b54f); ⌘Z and ⇧⌘Z find the stacks again only for a stack change (ced183f). A Library entry's or a collection's photos are stacked by their index IDs, without looking each up by URL (6137fac). Remove from Stack, Split Stack and moving within a stack (d7b96c7 to 5b58024): Remove from Stack takes the selected photos out of their open stacks, Split Stack splits an open stack before the active photo, and ⇧[ and ⇧] (Library's own) or a drag move a photo within its open stack, each a journaled batch with Undo in Library's one order, in the menus and the palette, with `redlamp library stacks remove`, `split` and `move`; the order kept in each sidecar's `stack.position` and the index's `stack_position` (schema 9); Unstack now takes apart every stack holding a selected photo, as Lightroom's does; a burst reordered or split becomes a stack made by hand; scenario `library.stacks-order`. The filmstrip marks focus-stack suggestions as the grid does (c0f1ba7). Put Back keeps a photo's place in its stack, through the file batch's saved rows, and an older build's journal still undoes (93f4133, a6d4b68). A photo dragged within its open stack in the filmstrip, as in the grid, with its Undo (ef836313; scenario `library.stacks-filmstrip-drag`). A photo active in a stack the view had open stays active when its folder is shown again or at launch (bf706c50). | Library plan (2026-10-05); [LIB §5](library-findings.md#5-recommendations) |

That check runs inside the file operations' queue, so no other batch moves a copy in between, and copies the index no longer has are reported, moving nothing (04fe73b, 2ed4929); it reads every copy whole again, 109 groups (2.1 GB) in 5.6 to 8.7 s (edc65f8). | LIB-40 | Library Health: checks that each list the photos needing a decision, from the index, the store and the indexer's one read per file, with LIB-39's exact duplicates the first: a rule for raw and JPEG pairs (keep both by default), damaged files (a new `unreadable` index state, since a failed read is reported today and nothing is kept; empty files; files that end early), wrong extensions (a mismatch of families, renamed with the sidecar, `.xmp` and pair following) and missing photos; each shown only while it has findings, with its reason in words; proposals drawn apart and acted on only as a confirmed batch to the Trash, with Undo; Keep Anyway in `Definitions/Health.json`, by content key; `redlamp library health` | Do better | P4 | M | LIB-10, LIB-23, LIB-26, LIB-39 | Accepted (2026-10-06): the owner asked to fold the Katami research into the catalog work | In progress (on main since 2026-10-10): built in the library and the command line (c5a7539 to 6420581). A file whose read fails is kept as `unreadable` with the reader's reason (schema version 6) and left out of every list, search and facet unless `unreadable:` is named; empty files, files no format starts and files that end early stay in lists and are found. Wrong extensions are found from the first bytes and fixed by a rename with the sidecar and `.xmp`. Raw and JPEG pairs follow a rule (both, raw or JPEG), halves with decisions of their own listed apart; exact duplicates are the first check. Each check is a source following diffs, offered only with findings; acting is one checked batch with Undo; Keep Anyway is in `Definitions/Health.json`, surviving a rebuild; `redlamp library health`. At a million photos the checks take 0.1 to 182 ms. Pairs follow the store's diffs, 0.42 to 0.53 ms after a one-photo change at a million (73cb230); files Redlamp may not read are listed and proposed for nothing (6d1a6e9). Library Health's checks in the Library panel (1bcc5350), each with its count and its photos as a source. In the app (3fd60b3 to e2d2f6a): each check's proposals drawn in the grid as a pill and a dashed frame on the photos a batch acts on, legible at every size; Accept Health Proposals… confirms a check's batch in a sheet that says what it does, never acting on a photo that's rated, flagged or labelled, through the file journal with Undo and Put Back; Keep Anyway, kept by content key and modification date, and List Again; Exact Duplicates confirmed in the background; each in Undo's one order; scenario `library.health`. Release at 20,000: scrolling with proposals at p99 0.9 to 5.4 ms, the sheet on screen in 35 to 38 ms, planned in 4.5 to 4.8 s, a 10,000-photo batch at p99 6.5 to 11.6 ms. The Unreadable filter is `is:damaged` (7ded590, 81ce0a2): what the Damaged Files check lists, in the filter bar's completion and a Damaged button beside Missing and Offline, smart collections and the CLI. A swept photo's findings go with it, and until the sweep the damaged-file and wrong-extension checks leave a removed root's photos out (7bfb30b). `is:damaged`'s first search after a change at p95 2.6 to 3.0 ms with 11,000 damaged, from 135 to 170 (b527caf). The Undo of a 10,000-photo batch rebuilds the menu bar 5 times rather than 40, at a main-thread p99 of 7.7 ms against 8.3, from 11.4 to 14.8 (0bc2e16). Missing photos (DEC-59), built (3516ad5 to 41c9f65): kept with everything decided about them, listed only by Library Health's Missing Photos with where each was and when it went; Locate… relinks a photo to a file with its content and offers the others found beside it; Remove from Library; each a journaled batch with Undo; the filter bar's Missing button gone; scenario `library.missing-photos`. Not done: `is:damaged`'s first search in a brand-new store at p95 18.4 to 18.9 ms against 16. Files that can't be read now show in their folder's grid, marked Unreadable or No Access, with Library Health's actions in their menu, and are counted in Folders and the folder's summary (0753432b, fb767459; scenario `library.unreadable-in-folder`). | The owner (2026-10-06); [Katami §3](notes/LIB-katami.md#3-library-health) |

| LIB-44 | The column store mapped from a snapshot: its columns, sort orders and the small tables' names saved beside the index in one page-aligned file, each column on a 16 KB boundary as it's laid out in memory, written atomically after batches of commits and at quit with the index generation it reflects; mapped copy-on-write at launch when its generation and schema match the index's, built from SQLite as today otherwise; later writes copy only the pages they touch; read ahead with one request on a cold launch. Done when a warm launch at a million photos searches without building the store, and memory over launch and idle after a trim stays within 250 MB and 120 MB with the store's pages left to the file cache | Build | P4 | M | LIB-06 | Accepted (2026-10-07): the owner's choice from the Cling study | In progress (on main since 2026-10-10): built (704eadc to b9f81f7). The store is saved beside the index as `Index.columns`, one page-aligned file: a 16 KB header (format, page size, schema version, a generation with a random token, each section's checksum), then each column, the sort orders and the small tables' names on 16 KB boundaries, 85 MB at a million. It's written atomically after a build, once the store and index have been quiet for 10 s (at most five minutes apart) and when asked, only the header when just the generation moved. At launch it's mapped copy-on-write when its generation and schema match, read ahead with one request when cold; one from another generation, schema, format or page size is deleted, a truncated or damaged one set aside, and the store built from SQLite. At a million photos a relaunch is searchable in 106 and 224 ms, from 505 and 509, and memory over launch and idle after a trim is 28 to 32 MB, from 225 to 236. The app saves it as it quits and trims the engine under memory pressure (5b47fb3). The store leaves a marked root's photos out, in a snapshot saved mid-removal too (3d6bec9). Not done: a run with the file cache emptied. | [LIB-cling §4](notes/LIB-cling.md#4-the-column-store-mapped-from-a-file-lib-05-lib-06) |

## 14. iPhone and iPad

The iPhone work (13 October 2026): before the app is built, a design study decides how Redlamp works on a phone, and the harness previews every phone screen, token and component on the Mac and on the phone (DEC-75). The app opens on the iPhone's Photos library, with its albums and filters, and a tap opens a photo to edit. iPad follows the same path once the iPhone's design is decided. The apps themselves stay in Phase 5 (DEC-20) until the owner decides otherwise.

## 15. Recorded skips

Decisions not to do something, kept so they aren't reopened without new evidence.

| ID | Skip | Why | Source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SKIP-01 | User-reorderable processing modules | darktable's own manual recommends against changing the order | [DT §5](darktable-findings.md#5-modules-filters-and-masks) |
| SKIP-02 | Generic module instances and blend modes | Mask layers are Redlamp's instances | [DT §5](darktable-findings.md#5-modules-filters-and-masks) |
| SKIP-03 | Multiple tone mappers, a second white-balance step or a second denoiser as separate panels | darktable's most confusing designs | [DT §4](darktable-findings.md#4-color-science-and-tone) |
| SKIP-04 | Database-first edit storage | Causes darktable's sync crawler and conflict dialogs | [DT §6](darktable-findings.md#6-edits-presets-styles-and-versioning) |
| SKIP-05 | Binary parameter blobs in sidecars | Unreadable and unmergeable | [DT §6](darktable-findings.md#6-edits-presets-styles-and-versioning) |
| SKIP-06 | Chorded shortcuts, exposed performance and GPU tuning, separate CPU code paths | Complexity without user value on unified-memory Apple Silicon | [DT §8](darktable-findings.md#8-architecture-performance-and-ux) |
| SKIP-07 | Liquify, projection changes, lens "simulate" mode | Not in Lightroom; low value | [DT §7](darktable-findings.md#7-lenses-and-geometry) |
| SKIP-08 | rawspeed, the lensfun library, darktable data files, AMaZE and RCD | Licenses don't fit an MPL-2.0 App Store app | [DT §9](darktable-findings.md#9-licensing-of-reusable-pieces) |
| SKIP-09 | PatchMatch-based healing | Adobe patents active to about 2031 | [AI §6](ai-findings.md#6-d-object-removal-healing-and-distraction-removal) |
| SKIP-10 | Diffusion upscalers and generative fill | Hallucination risk; non-commercial or restrictive licenses | [AI §4](ai-findings.md#4-b-super-resolution-and-upscaling) |
| SKIP-11 | Sky replacement and relighting | Not in Lightroom; conflicts with truthful editing | [AI §8](ai-findings.md#8-f-other-opportunities) |
| SKIP-12 | Studying vkdt for now | Owner decision, 30 September 2026 | Conversation |
| SKIP-13 | Face-specific generative restoration (GFPGAN, CodeFormer, RestoreFormer, face diffusion) | Changes identity details (in the bake-off, a dark eye turned blue and stubble appeared); every model is trained on FFHQ (non-commercial) | [H §6.4](notes/H-topaz-upscale-sharpen.md#64-faces) |
| SKIP-14 | libgphoto2 for tethering (darktable's camera library) | LGPL-2.1, and its maker tables are reverse-engineered data, both excluded by the clean-room policy | [TC routes](notes/TC-routes-and-licences.md) |
| SKIP-15 | A cloud review service like Capture One Live | Redlamp has no cloud; clients see captures on a second display or an iPad on the local network (TET-16) | [TC teardown](notes/TC-capture-one-teardown.md#review-and-clients) |
| SKIP-16 | Makers' camera SDKs for tethering (Sony Camera Remote SDK, Canon EDSDK, Nikon SDK, Fujifilm Camera Control SDK) | The owner's decision (2026-10-05, DEC-29): nothing from makers' SDKs; Redlamp's own code on Apple's frameworks and open protocols. The SDKs can't be in an MPL-2.0 repository, compete with macOS's `ptpcamerad` over USB, and Sony's and Fujifilm's require a warranty notice to users | [TC routes](notes/TC-routes-and-licences.md#4-licence-matrix) |
| SKIP-17 | Re-measuring noise reduction's Bayer noise table through the Menon demosaic (it was measured through Malvar's, which Menon replaced the same day) | Measured on 7 October: at equal texture kept it scores within 0.1 dB of the current table on the DN-11 scenes and the RawNIND Bayer pairs; it only makes each Luminance setting weaker, and the sliders were tuned on the current table. The calibration check now measures Bayer through Malvar | [DN-11 §9](notes/DN-11-lightroom-raw-denoise.md#9-follow-up-the-bayer-noise-table-skip-17-7-october) |
| SKIP-18 | A catalog file as the library's source of truth, with an import step that hides the folders (Lightroom Classic's model) | The owner's decision (2026-10-05, DEC-42): folders on disk organise photos and each photo's metadata stays in its sidecar, so nothing is locked in a catalog | The owner (2026-10-05) |
| SKIP-19 | A shared multi-user database, or real-time multi-user sessions | The owner's decision (2026-10-05): the index is per Mac and can always be rebuilt (DEC-42), and sidecars let a team share the files | [LIB §5](library-findings.md#5-recommendations) |
| SKIP-20 | Several catalogs, and merging them | The owner's decision (2026-10-05): a library of folders rebuilds its index and has nothing to merge (DEC-42) | [LIB §5](library-findings.md#5-recommendations) |
| SKIP-21 | Live two-way sync with other apps' catalogs | The owner's decision (2026-10-05): their private formats would need an update for each of their releases; Redlamp reads a copy instead (LIB-29, LIB-30) | [LIB §5](library-findings.md#5-recommendations) |
| SKIP-22 | Recovering Fujifilm's film simulations from camera firmware | Reversed (2026-10-14) by DEC-84: the firmware-derived model is offered as an experiment behind a toggle (TON-49), its data kept out of the repository (TON-50). The owner's decision of 2026-10-12: the firmware's licence and anti-circumvention law make it a risk, the image processor's tables are undocumented, and Fujifilm's published LUTs already cover ten simulations (DEC-73) | This tracker |
